# Allbert v1.2.6 -> v1.3.0 Request Flow

Status: **Stage 1 shipped; Stage 2 Memory/Search implementation, focused rejoin,
portability remediation, and M9.b.3 intent/direct-answer work are complete.
Earlier attended FOV rows exposed admission, durable-report, and semantic-
quality defects; their RED evidence remains recorded in Build Progress. The
five-model ladder is closed and changes no default.

M9.b.6 supersedes the M9.b.4.3/M9.b.5.3 phase-separated critic topology and
removes it while unreleased. `CriticAgent`, `ReqLLMCritic`, `ReviewProtocol`,
`ReviewRound`, the Worker `ReviewRound`/`Revise` commands, the worker-quality
gate, and the `fanout_review` closed task chain are deleted with their owner
tests. A healthy DirectAnswer child now performs exactly one generation call and
terminalizes; the composer performs exactly one synthesis call and otherwise
stores the existing deterministic complete-child renderer. Accepted paths record
exactly one physical provider attempt per owner, not three or six; ReqLLM and
Jido retries stay disabled. Budget v3 replaces v2 geometry, same-endpoint child
calls serialize inside the plan deadline, and per-row attempt accounting is
recorded from a gate-owned counter so a failed row cannot hide the call it
consumed.

The content bar is parity rather than absolute correctness: fan-out must not
make an answer materially worse than the same model answering in one turn,
measured against a frozen single-turn control. Absolute answer correctness moves
to DirectAnswer task-head qualification. The control is frozen and reproducible
at answer SHA-256
`627293e6ea46ef2a9643a55f63d07cb0ecde8abc88093cd9d40a07f808455405`.

`bench-v13-fanout --profile direct_answer_local` is GREEN on the shipped default
`local_ollama/qwen2.5:7b`: five consecutive clean runs at manager `2/2`,
composition `7/7`, and exactly one generation and one provider call on every
row. The `--mixed-mistral` diagnostic comparison sits at `4/7` and is not a
shipping configuration; its residual is recorded as a ReqLLM structured-output
integration candidate in `docs/plans/v1.3-plan.md`.

Fresh agent FOV, attended operator FOV, full SV-0 through SV-9 source
validation, replacement exact-SHA package construction, packaged-host
evidence/parity, sole final clean-SHA aggregate rejoin, and protected promotion
remain pending. No agent/operator, package, or aggregate PASS is claimed.**
v1.2.6 packages the
license inventory/viewer and converts the TUI to a daemon-owned session before
v1.3.0 adds long-term memory and Search Central. Milestone acceptance, parallel
workstreams, serial barriers, and gate timing live in
`docs/plans/v1.3-plan.md`; this document fixes the end-to-end behavior and
failure contracts those milestones must prove.

Corrective identity note (2026-07-29): `v1.2.6` is the active Stage 1 binary;
`v1.2.5` remains a failed immutable attempt. Stable `v121` internal names and
historical tag/SHA/run references remain literal. Any unqualified
forward-looking `v1.2.5` Stage 1 behavior below is the unchanged foundation
contract now shipping as `v1.2.6`.

## Source Documents And Authority

- `docs/plans/v1.3-plan.md` — Purpose, milestones, seams, gates, and release
  evidence.
- `docs/plans/roadmap.md` and `docs/plans/future-features.md` — the
  operator-promoted v1.2.6 enablers and v1.3.0 flagship.
- ADR 0002 and ADR 0089 — Markdown authority, proposal consent, claim history,
  and temporal retrieval.
- ADR 0021 and ADR 0083 — advisory intent/planning, registered-action authority,
  durable Objectives fan-out, selective Jido workers, and report composition.
- ADR 0076 and ADR 0091 — final-artifact packaging and daemon-backed TUI
  ownership.
- ADR 0092 — Corpus and Search Central ownership, projection, scope, and
  deletion behavior.
- ADR 0006, ADR 0031, ADR 0040, ADR 0045, ADR 0059, ADR 0073, and ADR 0081 —
  Security Central, Settings Central, distribution, confirmation, channel
  trust, surface routing, and additive post-1.0 contracts.
- `docs/developer/test-strategy.md` — lane ownership, metrics evidence, and
  authoritative release-gate semantics.

Where an older ADR still describes embedded TUI fallback, mutable memory
frontmatter, role-based authorship, or conditional memory-owned FTS, the
amendments named above and this active release triad supersede it.

## Release-Train Shape

```text
stable v1.2.6
  |
  +-- shipped WS-L: license catalog -> artifact finalizer -> packaged viewer
  |
  +-- shipped WS-T: Attach v1 additive session -> daemon service -> thin TUI
  |                         \
  +--------------------------+-- exact-artifact rejoin -> v1.2.6 binary
                                                   |
                                                   v
                 Conversations.Corpus + source policy + Jobs.Managed
                              /                         \
                             v                           v
                  Memory claims + proposal store     Search projection/API
                             \                           /
                              +-- privacy/eval rejoin --+
                                                   |
                                                   v
                 intent manager -> typed plan -> durable child Objectives
                              -> bounded workers -> durable composition
                                                   |
                                                   v
                                            v1.3.0 binary
```

License and TUI implementation may proceed independently. Their first serial
barrier is the frozen final release layout: licensing finalizes only after the
dispatcher and every other payload are present, and packaged TUI validation
uses those same finalized bytes. v1.3 implementation did not begin until the
v1.2.6 binary acceptance barrier closed. In v1.3, Memory and Search may proceed
in parallel only after Corpus, identity, consent, and managed-job ownership are
fixed and proven.

## System-Wide Invariants

1. `AllbertAssist.Conversations` is the sole canonical owner of conversation
   content and source authorization. Consumers use `Conversations.Corpus`; they
   do not read conversation tables directly.
2. Memory and Search are independent consumers. Memory never reads Search or
   FTS, Search never reads Memory, and neither grants authority.
3. Surfaces render and dispatch typed registered actions. Web, TUI, CLI, and DMs
   do not own search SQL, memory policy, confirmation state, or job loops.
4. A message role is not an identity. Only a verified mapped principal may be
   classified as the operator; assistant authorship remains distinct.
5. Projection data is disposable. Canonical conversations and Markdown claim
   streams remain authoritative.
6. Source/claim eligibility, digest, state, and authorization are rechecked when
   a proposal is reviewed, a Memory candidate enters a prompt, and a Search
   result is disclosed. A stale projection never becomes a stale disclosure.
7. Consent is consumer-, origin-, and source-class-scoped. Routine approved
   background work does not reprompt per run; expanded scope and destructive
   operations do.
8. All effects still resolve through `Actions.Registry` and execute through
   `Actions.Runner.run/3`. Security Central remains the authority boundary.
9. Every data/schema/protocol change is additive. Legacy Attach requests and
   pre-v1.3 memory files retain their existing meaning.
10. No flow below introduces a generic source adapter, event bus, projection
    framework, query DSL, scheduler, license service, or encryption subsystem.
    The one carve-out is `AllbertAssist.Projection.PromoteProtocol` (plan
    LD 62): it owns a single checkpoint/close/reopen/swap/verify sequence shared
    by the Memory and Search projections and holds no schema, verification
    predicate, generation metadata, or rebuild source. Implementing that
    sequence twice would duplicate the one protocol whose failure mode is silent
    data loss.
11. An Engine/classifier/Router action is a proposal, not final selection.
    Engine itself cannot emit an action-shaped decision for an ordinary false-
    or unresolved-evidence classifier/ranker/model candidate. Effectful Jido
    consumers (`IntentAgent` and the Objectives default lifecycle) also resolve
    the active descriptor's canonical `selection_policy`; advisory diagnostics
    cannot weaken either boundary. A semantic model/ranker proposal needs a
    positive descriptor-owned operator-act phrase at the start after bounded
    polite scaffolding; a contiguous phrase elsewhere and fuzzy/ordered ranking
    affinity are not evidence. Wrapped, negated, referential, and supplied-text
    mentions reject. Required slots alone authorize only when the descriptor
    explicitly sets validated
    `vocabulary.allow_required_slot_selection: true`, whose default is `false`;
    no current v1.3 descriptor opts in unless a frozen-corpus compatibility case
    is individually reviewed and recorded. A deterministic semantic predicate
    remains its own route evidence. `:explicit_evidence` uses the same lexical-
    start/operator-act boundary, so tokenization cannot strip a quote, code/list
    marker, escape, bracket, or other wrapper into an operator act; slots alone
    never satisfy it, including on matching deterministic ladder routes. An
    explicit deterministic `route_decision`/route hint is already selected by
    the typed ladder, so Engine annotates it; its resolved semantic predicate is
    evidence, while explicit-policy routes are still rechecked at the consumer.
    This compatibility exception never authorizes an ordinary ranked proposal.
    Selection phrases, descriptor negative vetoes, and clarification categories
    scan Unicode letters/numbers and treat punctuation, symbols, and emoji as
    boundaries, so punctuation cannot bypass a negative veto or distort a
    category match. The lexical-start check still uses the original text.
12. Clarification is structurally and canonically bounded. One malformed option
    rejects the whole proposed shortlist; otherwise only its grounded canonical
    subset is rendered and persisted. Descriptor category vocabulary may ground
    presentation but not direct execution. A reply must resolve one persisted
    option, which is revalidated against the original prompt through the current
    canonical clarification contract before ordinary parameter, Registry,
    Runner, Security Central, and confirmation gates. A rejected/empty proposal
    uses the consumer's clean canonical `direct_answer` path, carries no rejected
    slots/resource metadata, and cannot execute another proposed action. If the
    bound action lacks required parameters, its normal clarification persists;
    the third operator turn supplies them and resumes the same action through
    the normal gates rather than starting a fresh intent route.
13. A model/Router `:none` is advisory. Ordinary input receives a fresh validated
    `direct_answer` action; only the canonical deterministic `InputGuard` may retain the
    terminal no-match response for slash-command or internal-action-shaped
    input. Model diagnostics cannot manufacture that guard outcome.
14. `mix allbert <argv>` is the thin source twin of packaged `allbert <argv>`.
    Ordinary commands attach through the same CLI/API boundary before any
    embedded fallback; `serve` and `tui` delegate to their existing launchers.
15. Broad regex/list-shape Stage-0 classification is not adaptive fan-out
    authority. After special forms and exact counted offline protocols, a turn
    that reaches clean DirectAnswer uses one qualified conversation-model call
    to return one useful answer, closed evidence for every admission rule, and
    inert candidate children. A private Jido Agent owns the assess/adjudicate
    lifecycle, but adjudication is local and model-off: one declarative rule
    catalog derives the system instructions and deterministic policy criteria
    for semantic coverage, independence, advisory/read-only scope, supplied-text
    ownership, child-result dependency, parent-only join guidance, and useful
    parallel leverage. The compiler validates schema, original-request binding,
    bounds, and forbidden authority fields. A second provider call is allowed
    only to repair malformed or internally inconsistent evidence; it is not a
    routine judge. Uncounted effectful or mixed work stays one turn. A failed or
    uncertain plan uses the assessment's answer or ordinary DirectAnswer path
    without framing partial work.
16. Exact counted protocols and manager proposals converge on one canonical
    plan/compiler/framing path. The model cannot name an action, agent module,
    permission, identity, worker kind, confirmation result, dependency, or
    delivery route. Planner-generated child prose never amplifies authority;
    conversation-manager children are DirectAnswer-only. An exact-counted
    child may select another action only from an exact span of the digest-
    verified operator text, and it still crosses Registry, Runner, Security
    Central, and confirmations. A malformed or count-mismatched exact protocol
    is a typed fail-closed result and cannot fall through to manager planning.
17. Objectives remains durable fan-out truth. The existing non-DirectAnswer
    Lifecycle path and a temporary bounded Jido worker that delegates through
    the registered DirectAnswer action are two Adapters at one Worker Seam;
    validated action identity and original-request provenance select between
    them. The DirectAnswer worker is one-turn and adds no iterative tool loop.
    It performs exactly one generation call and then terminalizes; no model
    judges model output. Live Jido spawn/monitor/await state is
    reconstructible and cannot replace parent/child rows, Scheduler capacity,
    terminal transitions, cancellation, or delivery receipts.
18. Fan-in is two-phase. Deterministic terminal reduction freezes every child
    status/result/effect receipt, each applicable DirectAnswer child-quality
    receipt digest, and the
    join outcome while report delivery remains `not_ready`. Layout v1 remains a
    read/replay contract for historical reports. Every new layout-v2 selection
    comes from one qualified relationship/advisory generation call, validated
    deterministically against the exact completed-child partition.
    The deterministic Allbert renderer still owns headings, status, failures,
    authoritative child detail/receipt text, appendix order, bounds, and
    delivery. Sections and review positions exactly partition completed
    children; failed/cancelled/abandoned children stay outside model control.
    The stored body or truthful deterministic fallback precedes delivery
    `pending`; versioned input/selection/body digests bind all normalized
    provenance and synthesis bytes. Advisory prose cannot revise child truth,
    hide failure, claim effect evidence, execute an action, or create work.
19. Manager/admission observability is content-free and additive. The existing
    conversation action-log diagnostics may persist one allow-listed manager
    fact and one allow-listed admission fact. They expose only closed outcomes,
    rule/join classifications, bounded attempt/work-unit counts, reviewed state,
    and admitted/rejected/fallback/shadow disposition. They never persist the
    operator prompt, answer, proposed children, provider payload, or raw error,
    and they do not create a new event, table, logger, or authority path.

## Adaptive Fan-Out Flow (M9.b.4/M9.b.5, amended by M9.b.6)

```text
canonical operator turn
  -> steering/Search/notification special forms?
       yes -> existing owner
       no  -> exact counted offline protocol?
                malformed/mismatched -> ordinary manager-off single turn
                yes -> deterministic plan Adapter
                no  -> existing intent/action router
                         -> non-DirectAnswer route: existing behavior
                         -> clean DirectAnswer: one structured assessment call
                              -> private Jido local policy adjudication
                                   valid -> ordinary answer or typed plan
                                   malformed/inconsistent -> at most one repair
  -> append content-free manager/admission facts to existing action_log
  -> deterministic plan compiler
       reject/uncertain/dependent -> same-call answer / ordinary DirectAnswer
       overflow -> existing complete-list clarification
       accept -> atomically frame parent + ordered children + compact digest
  -> existing kickoff custody and acknowledgement
  -> Objectives Scheduler
       non-DirectAnswer -> existing Lifecycle action Adapter
       DirectAnswer     -> bounded temporary Jido worker Adapter
                            -> admitted against its local endpoint identity
                            -> registered-action model generation (exactly one)
                            -> terminalize; no model judges model output
                            -> receipt + accepted answer | failed child
     (all effects still Registry -> Runner -> Security -> confirmation)
  -> atomic terminal reduction freezes child envelopes + applicable
     DirectAnswer quality-receipt digests + join outcome
  -> report composition queued; delivery still not_ready
     -> one qualified structured model synthesis (exactly one call)
       -> drop sections carrying no queue position, then validate
       -> success: validate exact completed-child partition + join coverage
                   -> deterministic structure + advisory synthesis + appendix
          failure: persist deterministic complete-child fallback
     (failed/cancelled/abandoned children always render first)
  -> bind source + normalized selection provenance in report_selection_digest
  -> report delivery pending -> existing canonical surface/outbox path
       missed signal / TUI bus restart -> reconcile durable pending state
```

M9.b.6 is executable at clean pushed `dc1d4647` and supersedes the
M9.b.4.3/M9.b.5.3 phase-separated contract described in earlier revisions of
this section. FanoutManager resolves and Disclosure-authorizes only the closed
`fanout_manager` chain. Under one resolved Settings snapshot, Runtime separately
preflights and Disclosure-authorizes `direct_answer` and `fanout_synthesis`
before Budget v3 is computed or any parent/child Objective is durably framed.
`direct_answer` is the registered child generation route and `fanout_synthesis`
resolves the joined prose. The `fanout_review` chain is removed: no role
resolves a critic, because no model judges model output. Missing exact role
readiness returns the ordinary single answer with a content-free exact-role
diagnostic and no per-turn prompt. Once framed, a generation or synthesis
failure is an honest unresolved child or a deterministic fallback report, not
silent fallback to unchecked success.

Production manager and synthesis requests derive a domain-separated digest from
the exact redacted role/profile/provider/model/effective endpoint, closed
transport controls, response schema, and generation phase used at the physical
attempt. Manager repair binds the ordered attempt set. Synthesis generation
configuration is validated inside the composer boundary and is not added to the
frozen public selection carrier. Injected model clients are marked fixture
evidence and cannot substitute for production configuration provenance. Model
Doctor renders every closed role chain, its exact resolved/unavailable role,
`auto-pull=false`, and only fixed redacted diagnostic codes/messages; it never
emits a credential, raw provider body, arbitrary exception, or unredacted
endpoint.

Verification is deterministic because no model reviews model output. Acceptance
rests on properties Allbert already owns: child status totals, the ordered
authoritative appendix, receipt labelling, the exact completed-child partition,
byte bounds, and the frozen selection digest. The composer's structured-output
contract carries the meaning of every closed choice — each relationship value is
defined, and the positions array states that sections partition the completed
children with each child appearing exactly once. A returned section carrying no
queue position is dropped while normalizing raw provider output, because it
describes no child and removing it changes nothing the report covers; every
section that does claim a position faces unchanged validation, and a response
whose sections were all empty still fails closed.

Concurrent child model calls that resolve the same local endpoint identity are
admitted one at a time inside the existing plan deadline. This is a pure
admission predicate over an already-resolved binding — no scheduler, queue,
store, or supervision child — and hosted endpoints keep existing concurrency.
Admission decides only *when* a call may be attempted, never whether it is
permitted; Registry, Runner, Security Central, and confirmations are unchanged.

ReqLLM is locked at 1.18.0, whose upstream retry fix passes caller options into
the non-streaming retry step. Every fan-out-owned manager, generation, and
synthesis request sets `max_retries: 0` at both the Jido and ReqLLM boundaries.
Focused fault injection observes one physical adapter invocation for a
retryable error. A persisted `provider_call_count` of 1 therefore means exactly
one attempted provider request rather than a smaller count of framework calls
hiding retries. Attempt accounting is recorded against a counter the owner holds
rather than a count the result reports about itself, so a row that fails after
its call cannot hide that call and a fabricated self-reported count cannot pass
a row.

Before framing, Budget v3 reserves `manager_attempts + child_count *
worker_attempts_per_child + 1` calls and `manager_attempts * 1024 + child_count
* 3072 + 4096` output tokens. Defaults stay `64` calls and `32,768` output
tokens; no new setting appears and no operator value changes meaning. Calls and
output tokens are structural reservations and are not refunded; elapsed time is
one unchanged monotonic hard deadline, not a summed reservation or completion
guarantee. A plan that cannot fit takes the ordinary single-answer path.

Budget v1 and v2 rows are immutable historical contracts, not candidates for an
in-place upgrade. On first recovery under v3, a queued, claimed, running, or
dispatch-uncertain DirectAnswer child framed under a retired budget version
makes zero new provider calls and idempotently becomes failed/unresolved with
closed reason `quality_protocol_upgrade_required`; completed terminal children
remain replayable. A joined, queued-composer, composing, or mixed legacy parent
selects the existing complete-child deterministic fallback and also makes zero
provider calls. Repeated recovery may not duplicate terminal events, reports, or
delivery. No old row is rebound to Budget v3 or given a retroactive receipt; an
operator retry creates a new plan and leaves the historical row inspectable.

The canonical plan is versioned and stores only bounded execution intent:
`plan_source=counted_protocol|conversation_manager`, and each ordered child
carries a title, objective, and expected result. The parent objective plus those
child rows are the durable plan; compact version/source/digest provenance uses
existing bounded metadata and the `fanout_proposed` event instead of a duplicate
plan JSON column. Validated action identity selects the Worker Adapter; neither
the planner nor a surface may supply it.

When the final child terminalizes, the parent stores one frozen result envelope
per child: child id/title, terminal status, bounded observation or failure
reason, effect-receipt reference where one exists, and the verified quality-
receipt digest for each new completed DirectAnswer worker. Non-DirectAnswer
children retain the existing registered-action/effect-evidence contract and do
not invent semantic-review receipts. A historical DirectAnswer completion with
no replay-valid quality receipt stays readable but forces the explicit
`legacy_unreviewed_children` deterministic fallback; it is never silently
upgraded to reviewed evidence. A fan-out with no completed child has no accepted
observation to synthesize, makes zero provider calls, and stores deterministic
v2 fallback `no_completed_children/not_run`. Every persisted non-DirectAnswer
action name is re-resolved through the central Action Registry before v2 calls
it `registered_action`; unknown identity fails closed, but an already-selected
v1 report replays through its frozen contract without gaining this new
dependency. Completed observations require exact current-Step ownership plus a
completed Step. For cancelled, failed, or abandoned children, cancellation,
stale abandonment, and retry recovery may leave any schema-valid active or
terminal Step status; v2 verifies ownership/status, rejects completion and
quality-receipt authority, Registry-resolves any persisted non-DirectAnswer
action, and keeps the non-result outside synthesis. A missing Step is compatible
only with a nil `current_step_id`. Its appendix row displays terminal detail and
states that no completed result exists; a persisted action path does not promote
that detail to result authority, and a non-completed DirectAnswer row does not
itself force deterministic fallback. A known pre-fix v1.3 completed row with nil
`current_step_id` remains readable only when its exact event shape is
`{summary}`, `{summary, step_id}`,
`{summary, step_id, step_status=completed}`, or
`{summary, step_id, step_status=completed, quality_receipt}` and the referenced
Step, when one is named, has matching child ownership, completed status, durable
result summary, and any receipt binding. The summary-only compatibility shape
must exactly match the existing 500-character projection of the child's durable
Objective summary; because it names no Step, action, or receipt, it establishes
none of those authorities. Every compatibility shape is always legacy-unreviewed
deterministic fallback, never reconstructed reviewed/registered authority;
empty, extra, and unknown event shapes fail closed. A completed registered-action
child reuses the existing exact Lifecycle event with `summary` equal to the existing 500-
character Lifecycle projection of `child.last_observation_summary`, with that
durable Objective summary byte-equal to the completed Step's `result_summary`,
matching `step_id`, and `step_status=completed`, with no quality-receipt field;
the report layer does not accept a new summary-only substitute or mismatched
prose carrying action authority. The same transaction
sets `report_composition_state=queued` and keeps delivery `not_ready`. The
composer transition uses `not_ready | queued | composing | ready | fallback`.
Before any Lifecycle terminal path persists `current_step_id` or finalizes the
transient Step, it requires `step.objective_id == child.id`; this check also
applies while another sibling remains active and cannot be deferred to final
report freeze.
Historical layout v1 contains only relationship sections. New layout v2 adds
one normalized advisory-synthesis paragraph and closed review evidence over the
exact completed queue positions. The sections must partition completed
children, and two or more completed children require a meaningful multi-child
relationship; non-completed children never enter model-controlled sections.
The exact reviewed paragraph must fit unchanged after deterministic evidence
receives first claim on the report bound; otherwise composition selects an
unresolved deterministic fallback. Post-review shortening can never retain
accepted provenance.
Before attempting model rendering, Allbert computes the deterministic-fallback
child allocation. Relationship sections and advisory synthesis cannot worsen
any child's title/objective/detail shortening vector relative to that baseline;
if they would displace otherwise fitting authoritative evidence, model selection
is rejected and the baseline fallback is stored.
For the 16 KiB model input, the bounded parent request remains complete. With
optional child objective/expected-result fields held at their explicit marker
floor, Allbert first finds one largest common cap for every completed
observation. Values at or below 64 UTF-8 bytes remain complete; longer values
retain at least a readable 64-byte prefix plus the explicit marker. Only
remaining bytes expand optional task fields under a second
common cap. If all evidence prefixes cannot fit, no provider call occurs and
`composition_input_too_large/not_run` is stored.
The normalized model paragraph is always one fixed Markdown blockquote beneath
the Allbert-owned advisory label. This structural frame, rather than a brittle
substring/regex list, prevents prose from presenting as a deterministic heading
or child row; the closed review-rule catalog handles semantic presentation. The
synthesis digest covers the exact inner paragraph and rehydration validates the
exact frame before projection.
The exact raw model synthesis is also checked for Redactor-detectable secret
material before whitespace/control normalization. A match selects truthful
deterministic fallback; Allbert never redacts or mutates the prose and then
retains a review verdict or digest that covered different bytes.
Allbert renders deterministic failure/status/detail/receipt text and the
authoritative ordered appendix around the explicitly labelled advisory prose.
In layout v2 each appendix row names its authority class: reviewed advisory
rows display the verified quality-receipt digest and explicitly say it is not
effect evidence; completed registered-action rows say semantic review is not
applicable and retain the separate effect-receipt line; non-completed rows expose
terminal detail and say no completed result exists; completed legacy-unreviewed
rows expose the absent quality receipt and deterministic-fallback requirement.
Layout-v1 body
bytes remain unchanged. The operator-derived parent title plus child titles,
objectives, and observations/details are untrusted data, not receipt syntax.
Layout v2 deterministically JSON-string encodes every rendered occurrence before
Allbert's status, authority, quality-receipt, and effect-receipt lines; embedded
newlines and case variants of those labels remain visibly quoted data on model
and fallback paths. This structural boundary is reversible and does not depend
on a domain regex or another model judgment.
The plain `ReportComposer` owns one killable Task around the entire private Jido
synthesis lifecycle and caps it with the already-authorized effective composer
timeout. Jido `cmd timeout: 0` is used only inside that Task. Expiry terminates
the Task before selecting `synthesis_timeout/unresolved`, because the provider
boundary may already have been crossed; pre-dispatch exhaustion remains
`deadline_exhausted/not_run`. Unexpected Task exit stays crash-visible and boot
recovery records `recovery_after_restart/unresolved`, leaving no provider or
Jido child orphan.
Completion or fallback stores the report body/source, versioned input digest,
and `report_selection_digest` over normalized model/profile/provider/layout/
review/synthesis-digest provenance or closed fallback provenance, then moves report
delivery to `pending`. Boot recovery converts a stranded `composing` row to the
complete deterministic fallback rather than repeating an uncertain model
request and records `recovery_after_restart/unresolved`, since it cannot know
whether any bounded synthesis-phase dispatch began. Historical
pending/delivered v1 backfill stays on
its explicit byte-exact compatibility path. Compare-and-set storage and the
existing delivery receipt keep selected
report and transport idempotent. If an advisory join signal is missed, the
durable completion scan recovers pending work; if the TUI fan-out bus restarts,
the Adapter re-subscribes and reconciles its bounded attached-parent set from
durable projection state before displaying or acknowledging a report.

An unselected queued or stranded composing v1 input is never selected under a
new v1 contract. The existing immediate transaction first verifies the old v1
digest, derives the authority-bearing v2 input, and compare-and-swap rebinds the
same `report_input_digest` before claim or recovery fallback. Selected, pending,
or delivered v1 rows remain byte-exact. The append-only `fanout_joined` event is
not rewritten by that CAS: projection verifies its immutable digest against the
exact frozen v1 snapshot while parent/selection/body independently verify v2;
any other old-event mismatch stays inconsistent. Queue scan and recovery share
one explicit integrity-error classifier so a corrupt receipt, action identity,
or snapshot leaves only that parent untouched and does not head-of-line block
later valid compositions; unclassified database/operational failures still stop
the operation. Both paths cursor through deterministic 100-row batches and emit
at most one bounded content-free aggregate per typed reason per batch, with its
count, first/last id, and complete ordered `parent_ids` list. The batch cap
bounds the aggregate; a count or first/last id alone is insufficient.
The v2 queue boundary validates parent shape, terminal child shape, and the
absolute 16-child limit before issuing any Step/Event query. An oversized or
corrupt parent is therefore a classified per-parent skip, not an unbounded SQL
bind set or a head-of-line operational failure.

Manager planning runs inside the qualified DirectAnswer turn but resolves the
closed `model_preferences.tasks.fanout_manager` role. The composer uses
`model_preferences.tasks.fanout_synthesis`, and the registered DirectAnswer
child generation remains on
`model_preferences.tasks.direct_answer`. The new task defaults
inherit the existing `direct_answer_local` chain additively and are not claims
that its current model has passed the revised matrix. Fan-out-owned manager,
DirectAnswer-worker, and composition calls are structurally
reserved under Budget v3 against one plan-level call/token/elapsed-time budget
plus the existing global and per-fan-out run limits. The exact formula is
`manager_attempts + child_count * 6 + 6` calls and
`manager_attempts * 1024 + child_count * 3072 + 4096` output tokens. Existing
defaults become 64 calls and 32,768 tokens so the existing eight-child default
still fits; explicit operator values survive, and the unchanged 300,000 ms
value remains a hard monotonic deadline rather than a completion promise.
DirectAnswer is not automatically retried after an attempt starts because an
interrupted provider dispatch is uncertain; pre-attempt recovery and safely
registered non-model actions retain their existing recovery contracts. Child
action selection and recovered-step validation are deterministic and model-off; both
rederive the registered action and canonical parameters from authenticated
operator text. A counted child's registered action may normalize a resumable
confirmation packet. That action-owned packet is canonically bound to the
durable step when parked and rechecked at approval and immediately before
Runner, so legitimate normalization works without trusting mutable
confirmation YAML. The registered action also retains its own provider-use and
authority contract; the fan-out layer does not pretend to meter arbitrary
provider calls hidden inside an action.

The manager and Runtime also project bounded decision facts into the existing
assistant message's `action_log.diagnostics`. A manager fact names only its
closed result/outcome/policy/join classifications, attempts, work-unit count,
and reviewed state. An admission fact names only admitted, rejected,
single-turn fallback, or shadow-only plus a closed reason when applicable.
Signals, traces, and SQLite persistence all receive the same allow-listed form;
the operator prompt, useful answer, candidate children, provider payload, and
raw errors are excluded from those facts. The ordinary conversation answer
keeps its existing persistence contract. These facts diagnose whether a turn
stopped at manager
policy or failed later at Runtime framing without becoming execution authority
or a second durable plan.

# Part I — v1.2.5 Foundational Binary

## A. Final-Artifact License Flow

### A1. Reviewed inputs

```text
resolved OTP release application closure
  + mix.lock / resolved Hex metadata
  + ERTS/OTP and Elixir release inputs
  + known-extra catalog
      - SQLite/Exqlite amalgamation and NIF
      - OpenSSL only where copied into the target artifact
      - Castore code and Mozilla-derived CA payload
      - IANA timezone data
      - compiled/vendored web assets
      - staged plugins and packaged helper executables
      - dispatcher/runtime scripts
  + external-runtime declarations
      - Node / Playwright / Chromium
      - TradingAgents / Python packages
        (recorded external unless their bytes are actually present)
  |
  v
one reviewed declarative catalog + narrow exceptions
```

The catalog is the maintained knowledge boundary. It identifies known shipped
components, expected artifact paths or application names, license expression,
provenance, required license text, exact source-availability metadata, target
conditions, and whether the component is bundled or external. It is not a
recursive ownership database.

The Castore CA bundle is the one approved non-permissive exception: a narrow,
file-scoped MPL-2.0 record with exact Mozilla source provenance, payload digest,
license text, and an immutable exact-source retrieval reference with digest and
conversion provenance. Absence of that source-availability path fails the
build. External publication qualification performs an unauthenticated fetch of
the immutable reference and matches the recorded source digest. The
schema-v1 catalog selects the immutable-reference route and rejects companion
mode; a later schema may add companion packaging additively. The generator and
viewer remain offline. It does not admit MPL for arbitrary dependencies.
OpenSSL 3 is recorded under Apache-2.0 only on targets where the
release workflow copies its library into the artifact. SQLite remains recorded
as public domain.

### A2. Deterministic offline generation

```text
catalog + resolved build inputs
  |
  +-- reject missing known record / missing required text / disallowed license
  +-- reject known external runtime unexpectedly found in the artifact
  +-- canonical sort, path normalization, LF line endings, no clock/host paths
  |
  v
repo review products
  +-- THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.md       known cross-target union
  +-- deduplicated required texts  stable content-addressed set
  +-- deterministic check output  no network, Repo, daemon, or user Home
```

`mix allbert.licenses --check` regenerates in memory and fails on catalog/report
drift. It does not download license data during a gate. An unclassified arbitrary
byte can be reported as evidence, but only a missing or unexpected component at
a cataloged managed seam fails the release. The product claim is therefore
"best-effort inventory of known shipped components," not complete byte-level
attribution, a universal SBOM, or legal certification.

### A3. Final composition and per-target manifest

```text
clean stale Phoenix digest output
  -> compile/digest current web assets
  -> Mix release :assemble
  -> stage packaged plugins/helpers
  -> apply conditional macOS OpenSSL closure patch
  -> install the allbert dispatcher
  -> LICENSE FINALIZER                         LAST PAYLOAD MUTATION
       copy root LICENSE and minimal NOTICE
       copy THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.md + required texts
       inspect final target paths and resolved release closure
       emit THIRD-PARTY-MANIFEST.json
       verify known bundled/external decisions and orphan digests
  -> read-only artifact verification
  -> archive -> pre-promotion checksum + immutable workflow staging
  -> exact-artifact license/TUI qualification
  -> protected evidence-bound promotion gate
  -> rehash same archives -> cosign -> publish
```

The target manifest records target triple, exact resolved OTP/Elixir/ERTS
versions, known component/license entries, expected paths, required-text
digests, and bundle/external disposition. It omits host paths and generation
timestamps so rebuilding identical staged content produces identical license
outputs. The manifest may differ across targets because their payloads differ.

All three native builders use the same exact-version `erlef/setup-beam` path,
pinned by immutable action SHA. The macOS artifact is requalified once after
removing floating `brew update && brew install erlang elixir` provisioning. The
OpenSSL patch remains only if post-change dependency-closure evidence shows it
is still required.

### A4. Packaged viewer

```text
allbert licenses [summary|list|show <component>|notices|--json]
  |
  +-- top-level dispatcher recognizes `licenses` before Req/application startup
  +-- locate immutable files relative to the installed release
  +-- parser reads THIRD-PARTY-MANIFEST.json and packaged text files
  +-- output is stable, bounded, and pipe-friendly
  '-- no Req/Allbert runtime boot, Repo, daemon attach, regeneration, or network
```

The viewer is part of every binary, including a machine with no source checkout
or Elixir toolchain. Missing/corrupt packaged metadata returns a stable non-zero
diagnostic naming the artifact defect; it never falls back to workstation
dependency metadata or the network.

### A5. Exact-artifact reconciliation

Each native target is built once. The resulting finalized archive is the input
to license-viewer smoke, packaged TUI smoke, checksum/signature publication, and
install-path validation. The product-tag workflow stages versioned archives and
their pre-promotion checksums with `retention-days: 30`, then ends without
signing or publication only after its downstream no-build license/TUI
qualification jobs upload attempt-qualified digest-bound evidence artifacts in
that same source run. Every manifest binds repository, exact workflow file/id,
`event: push`, product tag/ref and source SHA, source run ID/attempt, immutable
artifact IDs/attempt-qualified names, and digests. Successful builder jobs do
not rerun for qualification-only corrections; `upload-artifact@v4` overwrite is
never used. Promotion must occur while every selected native artifact is no
more than 21 days past its GitHub artifact `created_at`; later qualification
attempts do not reset that clock. A separate
dispatch at the exact product-tag ref enters a job-level protected environment
before runner allocation; after approval it authenticates the run metadata and
supplied latest attempt, downloads archives/manifests by immutable artifact ID,
rehashes, and signs/publishes those exact staged bytes. It never rebuilds. A
partial retry creates the release only if absent, digest-compares and skips
identical assets, uploads missing assets, and stops on different bytes; it never
clobbers. Evidence reconciles:

- cataloged known union versus every target manifest;
- manifest paths and digests versus extracted final bytes;
- target-specific OpenSSL and external-runtime dispositions;
- current asset-manifest reachability versus staged digest files;
- `allbert licenses --json` versus the packaged manifest; and
- published archive digest versus the exact validated archive.

No gate rebuilds a lookalike artifact for validation.

## B. Daemon-Backed TUI Flow

### B1. Additive Attach v1 protocol

The existing Home-local Unix-domain socket, token, Home/user/version handshake,
packet bounds, and concurrent-client cap remain. The request shape gains an
optional request kind:

```text
kind absent or :command -> existing unary argv request/reply semantics
kind :tui_session       -> bounded authenticated session framing
unknown kind            -> stable unsupported-kind error
```

Existing clients that omit `kind` remain valid. The session extension is for
the TUI contract only; it is not a general remote transport or application
streaming framework.

The Erlang-term wire shape remains atom-keyed and atom-tagged in the existing
bounded safe-term codec; string aliases are not accepted and decoding never
creates atoms from input. Because `kind` is inside the shared first term, the
transport performs the legacy-compatible `[:safe]` decode needed to classify
that packet; a raw `COMPRESSED` packet classified as `:tui_session` is rejected
before session schema/identity validation or runtime dispatch. Later session
packets reject `COMPRESSED` before term decoding. Session open is
`%{kind: :tui_session, frame: :open, ...}`. After it succeeds, the closed
client-to-daemon frame set is `:input`, `:resize`, `:cancel`, `:confirmation`,
`:ack`, and `:detach`.
The closed daemon-to-client set is `:snapshot`, `:delta`, `:status`,
`:confirmation`, `:completion`, `:error`, and `:close`. Frames carry protocol
version, session id, and monotonic sequence where ordering matters.
Unknown/invalid-schema/wrong-session frames fail with `:protocol_error`, bodies
over 64 KiB with `:frame_too_large`, sequence gaps/regressions/exhaustion with
`:sequence_error`, and regressed/impossible acknowledgements with `:ack_error`;
they fail the session, not the daemon. An incoming `ack` acknowledges this
endpoint's outgoing stream and must remain between the last peer ack and the
highest local sequence sent. The existing Settings Central
`channels.tui.max_text_bytes` value is reused as both the inbound-input and
ordinary rendered-text ceiling (bounded 1–32,000); no second TUI text-limit
setting is added. The already-typed authority-only delivery path may instead
carry a central fan-out report through its closed 32,768-byte body bound, while
the unchanged 48 KiB `lines` and 64 KiB frame limits remain authoritative. This
exception is derived from the delivery envelope, not from text, and does not
expand operator input or ordinary presentation.
The root map counts as container depth one; only maps and proper lists are
session containers, and exact schemas reject tuples, floats, process/runtime
terms, and unknown keys. Frame validation is atomic and ordered: schema/session/
direction, then sequence, then acknowledgement, then bounded admission. Render
revision remains presentation correlation only; snapshot replaces, while v1
does not infer authority or a transport close from revision monotonicity.
ADR 0091's protocol-v1 table is normative for exact required/optional fields,
field types, frame/text/resize/decode bounds, sequence and cumulative-ack
windows, queue limits/drop order, correlations, snapshot/delta shapes, and
close/error codes. `binary_to_term(..., [:safe])` is followed by that application
schema/bounds validation; safe-term decoding alone is never acceptance.

Every admitted `:input` carries a client-generated bounded `input_receipt_id`
whose identity is independent of the transient session id and sequence. A
retry after an ambiguous close, including through a fresh authenticated open,
reuses that receipt id. Before **every** ordinary, slash, correction, and coding
`:input` dispatch, the daemon atomically gates through an extended
existing `channel_events` inbound row keyed by verified local operator/profile
plus receipt id. The row binds the normalized payload with a domain-separated
`allbert.tui.receipt-payload.v1` HMAC derived from
`secret://system/integrity_v1`/version `1` and records
admitted/running/terminal state plus input-signal/message and safe result refs.
A live retry receives running/completed state without another dispatch; a
different payload under the same id fails closed. If the daemon restarted after
admission but before a durable terminal ref, retry returns `outcome_unknown` and
requires an explicit new receipt to retry—it does not claim impossible exactly-
once effect recovery. The client creates the id before send and retains it until
a terminal acknowledgement. Frame sequence/
ack state provides bounded transport pressure only; it is never the durable
input-idempotency key.
The separate `:confirmation` frame remains idempotent through its existing
confirmation id and same-channel callback binding; it does not use an
`input_receipt_id`.

M0.b2 adds one narrow capability to existing Settings Secrets/Key Custody:
serialized `fetch_or_create_system/2` accepts only closed ref
`secret://system/integrity_v1`, generates 32 random bytes only for a genuinely
fresh Home, commits encrypted material before returning it, and never exposes
the value through ordinary settings output. The TUI derives only
`allbert.tui.receipt-payload.v1`; v1.3 reuses the same ref with distinct Memory,
Search, and delete-preview domains. The shared helper derives each domain key as
HMAC-SHA-256 of the Home key and UTF-8 domain string, then HMACs the canonical
length-prefixed ordered field list with that domain key. Its callable seam is
`system_hmac(domain, fields, key_version)` plus
`verify_system_hmac(domain, fields, tag, key_ref, key_version)`; the raw
fetch/create primitive remains private. There is no second key process,
file, or per-consumer encoding, and consumers receive tags/verification results
rather than raw Home key material.

As landed, a new receipt commits `received` and then `admitted` before handing
normalized input to the Adapter for classification and dispatch. A rejected
handoff is terminalized as failed. This is deliberately more conservative than
leaving an unrecorded classification/dispatch window and does not add a second
classification service.

### B2. Ownership and startup

```text
operator runs allbert tui
  |
  +-- resolve ALLBERT_HOME; read attach socket/token
  +-- socket/token absent
  |      -> fail closed with exact `allbert serve` / service repair guidance
  |         (never boot an embedded Runtime)
  +-- handshake mismatch
  |      -> refuse with Home/user/protocol/version diagnostic
  '-- authenticated open
         -> daemon reserves the Home's one TUI session
         -> run existing IdentityBootstrap.prepare_local_launch semantics
         -> explicit-false remains disabled; otherwise start exactly one
            temporary headless TUI adapter child
         -> duplicate open returns already_attached
         -> daemon emits initial snapshot
         -> client enters raw mode only after open succeeds
```

The daemon owns Runtime, operator identity, confirmations, Pi-mode state,
conversation persistence, action execution, and event fan-out. The client owns
TTY detection, input normalization, resize signals, rendering, and idempotent
terminal restoration on handled exits. SIGKILL, power loss, or terminal failure
cannot run cleanup; operator diagnostics provide `stty sane`/`reset` recovery
without a watchdog. The client sends no authority-bearing module/action
names supplied by model output; user input follows the existing Runtime and
registered-action paths.

Before authentication, the daemon permits at most 16 concurrent pending
handshakes, each with the existing five-second read deadline. This is separate
from the 16-child unary-command task cap. A successful open transfers the socket
to its monitored session owner and releases the pending slot; failure closes it
without creating an adapter or runtime work.

The normal daemon channel supervisor excludes the TUI adapter until an
authenticated session open; the session owner performs the same bootstrap that
the embedded launcher previously performed and starts one supervised temporary
child. Session close stops it; a later open repeats bootstrap/settings resolution
and starts a fresh child. This avoids both an unconditionally booted stale/
disabled adapter and a duplicate dynamically started adapter.

If the Attach listener cannot bind, `serve` and Web may remain available with a
visible unhealthy/degraded Attach status. TUI and attach-only CLI commands fail
closed with repair guidance; they do not start a competing embedded writer.

The M0.b2 session owner is a plain GenServer because it owns bounded transport
custody and lifecycle cleanup but no action authority or durable state machine.
M0.b3 excludes the static adapter from normal application startup and replaces
both launchers with the same attach-only thin client; neither `allbert tui` nor
its Mix twin has an embedded Runtime fallback.

### B3. Session lifecycle, pressure, and cancellation

```text
terminal input -> bounded client frame -> daemon session owner
  -> existing TUI Adapter / Runtime attended-turn FIFO
  -> confirmations and action effects through ordinary authorities
  -> ordered bounded event frame -> render
```

- One active TUI session per Home; no live frame-stream reconnect/replay in
  v1.2.5. A fresh session may inspect/retry an ambiguous input with the same
  `input_receipt_id`; it sees terminal/running state or `outcome_unknown` under
  the durable receipt contract above and never automatically redispatches an
  uncertain input.
- Resize and render events coalesce. Low-value progress may be dropped under
  pressure; confirmation, completion, error, and terminal-state frames may not.
- Daemon CRLF/LF output becomes bounded logical terminal lines, including blank
  lines. The client repeats that expansion defensively for all presentation and
  confirmation text, replaces every other C0/C1 control rather than executing
  it, and fails closed if required confirmation disclosure cannot fit.
- Droppability follows presentation priority/custody rather than the frame tag:
  an authority fan-out `:delta` keeps its waiter through gap recovery until
  cumulative client ack or teardown. A close drains already-queued
  non-droppable frames after applying the peer ack and is the final frame;
  bounded inability to drain fails custody and closes as overflow.
- A slow client cannot grow daemon memory without bound or block Runtime.
- Socket loss releases the session; a surviving client that detects it restores
  its own terminal.
- Durable background jobs already admitted may complete after disconnect.
- An attached, effectful Pi coding turn is cancelled fail-closed on session loss;
  no unseen confirmation or next effect proceeds.
- TTY restoration runs for normal exit, Ctrl-C, daemon error, decoder error, and
  abrupt socket closure.
- The packaged dispatcher and source qualification runner append OTP `+Bc`
  only for the thin-client VM. This delivers raw Ctrl-C to state-aware handling:
  interruptible attended states or unresolved client-custodied input cancel with
  `:operator_interrupt` before detach, while idle/error/durable-background state
  with no unresolved input detaches without a synthetic cancellation. It does
  not change daemon signal handling, invoke `stty`, or rely on private OTP
  terminal-mode APIs.
- Uncatchable client termination has explicit `stty sane`/`reset` recovery and
  no claim that client cleanup ran.

## C. v1.2.5 Release Rejoin

Immutable attempts `v1.2.1` at SHA `9f47c7c7`, `v1.2.2` at SHA `15c15484`,
`v1.2.3` at SHA `b8a01584`, and `v1.2.4` at SHA `2faaa426` produced no workflow
artifacts or GitHub Release.
The first exposed an unbound web asset-clean root; the second proved that nested
umbrella Mix structure can restore tracked logical gzip siblings after Phoenix
cleanup. The third completed all three native release builds, then its
early-exiting `awk` consumer closed `mix hex.info` stdout before Hex finished
emitting system information, causing `EPIPE` in every packaging step. The
fourth consumed that output correctly but rejected setup-beam's actual
`v1.19.5-otp-28` distribution label by incorrectly deriving `otp-29` from the
independently verified runtime major. The operator authorized the normal patch
recovery: retain all four failed tags,
keep bounded cleanup/catalog corrections in their existing seams, make the
inline toolchain parsers consume producer output to EOF, pin the observed
distribution label separately from runtime versions, and repeat this flow at
immutable tag `v1.2.5`. `release.v121`,
`v121-*` evidence identifiers, and harness filenames remain stable internal
contract names; every product archive, manifest, qualification row, promotion
input, installed-product check, and closeout statement identifies v1.2.5.
Nothing rebuilds or publishes under any failed tag.

The v1.2.5 candidate barrier requires one clean pushed SHA and the exact native
artifacts derived from it. Focused license and TUI lanes run during development;
the rejoin records docs/drift review, dependency-refresh disposition, focused
test/static/security metrics, and the structurally verified ordered-step
definition `release.v121 = release.v12 + v1.2.5 focused contracts`. It executes
no aggregate/full-project command: not `mix precommit`, `release.v1`,
`release.v12`, `release.v121`, `release.v13`, or the full release gate. Those
definitions remain composed so final M9.b can prove and execute the cumulative
contract at one exact v1.3 SHA.

After that focused source rejoin passes, the `v1.2.5` tag triggers exactly one
native build for each target. The resulting versioned archives and digest manifest are
retained for 30 days and held for separate packaged-license qualification and
TUI FV; no validation rebuild is allowed. Downstream jobs in that source run
consume the immutable archives, upload attempt-qualified artifact container
`m0a3-evidence-a<run_attempt>` with stable inner `m0a3-evidence.json`, then
compose attempt-qualified `qualification-evidence-a<run_attempt>` with stable
inner `qualification-evidence-manifest.json` after TUI FV; neither job uploads new
product bytes. Once that source run succeeds, dispatch the separate protected
`workflow_dispatch` promotion at the exact immutable tag ref while that per-
artifact window remains open. The promotion tag input must match
`GITHUB_REF_NAME`; it also
receives the source workflow run id/attempt plus exact digest/evidence artifact
IDs and digests. The `release-promotion` environment gates the
job before a runner or environment secret is available. After approval,
explicit `actions: read`, `contents: write`, and `id-token: write` permissions
let it authenticate repository/workflow/`push` event/conclusion/tag/SHA/attempt,
download by artifact ID, verify archive digests and evidence bindings,
rehash, cosign-sign, and publish those same bytes. It contains no build job.
Unchanged-byte qualification retries upload new attempt-qualified evidence and
reuse staged archives without rerunning successful builders. A source or
artifact-byte fix requires a new
operator-authorized immutable patch tag; missing, older-than-window, or expired
staging evidence stops for operator disposition and never silently rebuilds
under an accepted digest set. Tap fill, install, signature verification, and
re-smoke follow. A publication retry skips only digest-identical existing
assets, uploads missing ones, rejects differing bytes, and never uses
`--clobber`.

# Part II — v1.3.0 Memory And Search

## D. Canonical Corpus And Source Policy

### D1. SourceEnvelope production

```text
Memory collection or Search projector asks Conversations.Corpus.page/1
  |
  +-- canonical ownership query scoped to operator/Home and high-water bound
  +-- stable keyset cursor (inserted_at, stable message id)
  +-- verified channel/principal mapping and current source classification
  v
typed, versioned SourceEnvelope
  - stable message/thread/operator/principal identifiers
  - author kind and role as separate fields
  - source class, surface/channel, thread kind, and trust classification
  - canonical creation/order timestamp and current content digest/version
  - content and bounded origin/trace references
  - no permission or authority grant
```

M1 froze schema version `1` as a typed struct. Its required fields and enums
are plan LD 80; digests are `sha256:` plus 64 lower-case hex characters and
remote `origin` is the exact admission tuple rather than caller metadata.
`origin_scope` is the base `local_operator | mapped_operator_dm` transport
class and `origin_overlays` is closed to `[] | [e2ee_operator]`; the overlay
cannot erase which base scope must be granted.
Snapshots bind operator, policy, inclusive high-water `(inserted_at, id)`, and
`eligibility_epoch`. Pages are ascending keyset reads, default `200` and maximum
`500` rows; their cursor advances from the last **scanned** canonical row, not
the last emitted envelope, so filtered rows cannot loop or skip later rows.
Rehydration accepts at most `100` unique refs and preserves request order with
one envelope or typed unavailable reason per ref. Context is the
source plus at most four preceding and four following same-thread messages,
maximum nine messages/32,768 UTF-8 bytes; farthest context is dropped first and
truncation is explicit. If the authoritative source alone exceeds that byte
cap, `conversation_context` returns `:source_context_too_large`; canonical
envelope content is never silently truncated.

The high-water bound makes one scan repeatable while new messages arrive.
Opaque cursors are internal Corpus tokens, not table offsets exposed as public
contracts. Consumers persist only their own cursor/digest state; no generic
consumer cursor table is introduced.

Corpus also owns batch `rehydrate_and_authorize` and bounded
`conversation_context` reads. Search and Memory use those operations for final
eligibility/digest checks and nearby assistant disambiguation respectively.
They never join or attach the canonical conversation tables themselves.

Verified operator authorship requires the current principal mapping and trust
classification; `role == "user"` alone is insufficient. In one inbound
admission transaction, each canonical remote message stores nullable
`origin_thread_ref_id`, immutable normalized `origin_principal_digest`, and
`principal_normalizer_version`, while its provider message ref stores the exact
`thread_channel_ref_id`; Corpus compares that snapshot with the current enabled
identity mapping. A legacy remote row is backfilled only if one exact ref and
principal mapping is provable, otherwise it is
`legacy_principal_unverified` and excluded. Local authenticated Web/TUI/CLI rows
derive operator identity from canonical user ownership plus local trust and need
no fake provider ref. Visible assistant
replies copy the verified request origin evidence while retaining assistant
authorship and may be indexed by Search, but they cannot
originate or corroborate a memory claim.

### D2. Change notification without a new event platform

```text
canonical conversation commit/delete/remap
  -> existing signal path emits a content-free coalesced dirty wakeup
  -> Jobs.Managed.kick("search-index", Home) advances the existing entry's due
     time without creating a job or running Search directly
  -> the ordinary Jobs runner records and executes Search ingestion when due

hourly search-index repair
  -> capture the epoch's Corpus high-water, starting `eligibility_epoch`,
     and starting projection revision
  -> bounded cyclic Corpus inventory with an independent repair cursor/epoch
  -> compare canonical ids/digests/eligibility with projected rows
  -> upsert current rows and mark them seen in this repair epoch
  -> at a completed compatible epoch, sweep only rows whose immutable
     first_projected_revision <= the epoch-start revision and which were not
     seen/currently eligible
```

Canonical writes never wait for Search. A missed wakeup only affects reported
freshness: query-time Corpus reauthorization still prevents disclosure. There
is no durable event bus or cross-database transaction. The repair cursor is
Search-owned projection state and advances across bounded hourly runs; it does
not restart a full-corpus scan every hour or turn into a generic consumer
offset service. Writes after the epoch high-water remain dirty and cannot be
swept as unseen members of the older epoch. A pause on `search-index` suppresses
dirty kicks without clearing dirty state; resume, manual run, or the next hourly
due run catches up through the same Jobs entry and run history.

Corpus owns two independent monotonic boundaries: ordinary append advances the
source high-water, while canonical deletion/visibility change, principal remap
or invalidation, Search-grant revocation, and Search E2EE opt-out advance
`eligibility_epoch`. A later source high-water leaves compatible build work
valid and dirty for catch-up; an eligibility-epoch change invalidates a builder
whose authorized population may have shrunk or moved.

### D3. Feature grants and source classes

Memory consolidation and Search have independent, non-transitive Settings
Central grants, switched by two named keys with deliberately opposite defaults:
`memory.consolidation.enabled` (default **false**) and `search.enabled`
(default **true**, one switch over both query availability and the ordinary
local managed set). `private_operator` remains the trust/source class, but its
grant also carries one of the three frozen origin scopes — `local_operator`,
`mapped_operator_dm`, `e2ee_operator` — so local defaults cannot accidentally
authorize a less-private transport:

- `local_operator`: verified local Web/CLI/TUI origins. Search is implicit and
  default-on for this origin; Memory remains explicit and default-off.
- `mapped_operator_dm`: current and future verified mapped one-to-one operator
  DMs. One disclosed opt-in per consumer covers this origin—not one prompt per
  channel or thread. Search query scope still defaults to the current DM
  channel/thread under §F3.
- `e2ee_operator`: an additional audited per-consumer overlay on either origin.
  Search discloses that indexing creates a local plaintext-derived projection;
  Memory discloses that a reviewed proposal may become a plaintext Markdown
  claim.
- group/shared/programmatic/unknown: excluded in v1.3.

A Search grant never authorizes Memory collection; a Memory grant never
authorizes Search projection. Once the relevant consumer/origin grant exists,
ordinary eligible ingestion and recurring work do not ask repeatedly. The
canonical invalidation matrix is:

Grant mutation writes the Settings value before advancing that consumer's Repo
epoch. Corpus page reads check both the current grant and epoch before and after
materializing a page. This makes revocation fail closed across the intentionally
non-transactional Settings-file/Repo boundary without adding a cross-store
coordinator.

| Event | Memory proposals/claims | Search results/projection |
|---|---|---|
| canonical message/thread deletion | affected pending proposals/legacy drafts become content-free stale outcomes; kept claims survive with unavailable provenance | suppress immediately; dirty repair removes rows |
| principal invalidation or unverified remap | affected pending proposals stale; kept claims survive with unavailable provenance | suppress immediately; dirty repair removes rows |
| Memory origin-grant revoke or Memory E2EE opt-out | affected pending proposals stale; kept claims survive | unchanged |
| Search origin-grant revoke or Search E2EE opt-out | unchanged | suppress immediately; dirty repair removes rows |
| origin remapped and reverified | each consumer re-evaluates only under its own current grant | each consumer re-evaluates only under its own current grant |

An unverified principal/remap makes the source ineligible to both consumers;
consumer-specific grant changes affect only that consumer. No schedule or
projection row confers authority.

Already reviewed Memory claims remain separate operator-owned records until an
independent Archive or confirmed Forget action. Provenance reports that their
source is unavailable/revoked rather than fabricating accessible evidence.

### D4. Confirmed canonical conversation deletion

The third readiness pass found LD 50 and §K3 depending on a canonical delete
that does not exist in the tree; the fifth pass gave it its own architectural
home in **ADR 0093** and plan **LD 79**, because deleting the operator's
history is a Corpus-owned destructive capability rather than a search concern.
M2 adds one ordinary registered Conversations
action, `delete_conversation_content`, not Search or Memory functionality. Its
schema-1 request is `%{target_kind: :message | :thread, target_id: String.t(),
expected_digest: nil | "sha256:" <> lower_hex_64}`.
The authenticated canonical user/operator is server-derived and the target must
belong to it. The client digest remains optional, but the server preview always
derives and binds the exact conversation-owned cascade: message/thread
identities and row versions/content digests plus the ordered channel-thread/
message-reference row identities/versions that the Repo transaction will
delete, followed by a versioned ordered known-core survivor/blocker count
vector and disclosure version. Durable confirmation
stores safe user/operator id, target kind/id/counts, and non-secret key ref/
version plus an `allbert.conversations.delete-preview.v1` HMAC over that ordered
preview, never message text or plain content digests. Cross-domain dependents
are counted for disclosure but are not part of the cascade:

For the optional client precondition, a message digest is SHA-256 of its exact
content; a thread digest is SHA-256 of the NUL-joined, canonical-order list of
its per-message `sha256:` content digests. The server-bound preview remains the
stronger authority because it also covers the thread, references, counts, and
disclosure version. The serialized resume payload carries the server-derived
user id and approval compares it with the current authenticated user before
entering the deletion transaction.

```text
operator requests one exact message or thread target
  -> preview target kind/id, exact conversation-owned cascade, dependent counts,
     and narrow-copy consequence disclosure
  -> destructive confirmation through Security Central bound to that preview
  -> canonical delete COMMITS FIRST in Conversations
       message: delete that message and its canonical reference rows
       thread: delete the thread and cascade its messages/reference rows
  -> Corpus immediately reports the source missing/ineligible
       (query-time reauthorization now covers the whole repair interval)
  -> content-free Jobs.Managed search-index kick schedules projection removal
  -> pending Memory proposal/legacy-draft payloads sourced only from deleted
     messages are scrubbed to content-free stale outcomes
  -> independently kept Memory claims are NOT deleted
       provenance reports the source as unavailable until Archive or Forget
  -> content-free result
       schema version, target kind/id, deleted/already_deleted,
       deleted message/reference counts, retained_thread_title?,
       downstream_reconcile_required, disclosure version
```

The preview DTO is `%{schema_version: 1, target_kind, target_id,
message_count, reference_count, blocker_counts, survivor_counts,
retained_thread_title?: boolean, disclosure_version: 1, preview_binding}`. The
closed `blocker_counts` keys are `active_jobs`, `active_objectives`,
`nonterminal_fanout_deliveries`, and `open_workspace_states`; closed
`survivor_counts` keys are `historical_jobs`, `historical_runs`,
`historical_objectives`, `stocksage_rows`, `channel_events`,
`channel_deliveries`, `artifact_links`, `traces`, and
`closed_workspace_states`. Every value is a non-negative integer. Unknown
plugin copies are disclosed as uncounted, never encoded as a false zero.
`preview_binding` is `hmac-sha256:` plus 64 lower-case hex characters. The
result DTO is `%{schema_version: 1, target_kind, target_id, outcome: :deleted |
:already_deleted, deleted_message_count, deleted_reference_count,
retained_thread_title?: boolean, downstream_reconcile_required: boolean,
disclosure_version: 1}`. Typed pre-commit errors are `:not_found | :stale |
:live_dependency | :unauthorized`; safe blocker detail accompanies only
`:live_dependency`. Message row version is its immutable `inserted_at` plus
content digest; thread and reference versions are `updated_at` plus their safe
identity fields. The server binds those exact values rather than adding a
version column solely for deletion.

`delete_conversation_content` is `resumable?: true` and is explicitly added to
`ApproveConfirmation`'s existing generic-resume allowlist. The durable pending
confirmation id plus its server-bound preview is the retry key. If the process
stops after the Repo cascade but before confirmation resolution, retrying that
same pending approval finds the target absent, returns `already_deleted`, and
re-drives the idempotent/coalesced downstream stale-mark/Jobs kick. A fresh
ordinary request for an absent target returns `not_found`. The hourly reconcile
remains the no-retry backstop. No impossible YAML/Repo atomicity, receipt row, or
new deletion-ledger store is introduced. A
caller-supplied or server-bound digest
mismatch requires a new preview/confirmation rather than deleting changed
content. The action recomputes the full cascade digest inside the immediate Repo
transaction, including the bound known-core count/disclosure vector; a
concurrent message/ref/dependency change makes the confirmation stale rather
than expanding its authority. Message deletion recomputes
`conversation_threads.last_message_at` as remaining-message `MAX(inserted_at)`
or the thread's own `inserted_at` when none remain. A thread title
previously derived from that message is intentionally retained and disclosed—it
has no current provenance field and v1.3 does not add one merely to rewrite it.

Before thread deletion, a best-effort known-schema dependency inventory blocks
on live execution-bearing references (active origin-thread Jobs/objectives,
nonterminal fan-out delivery, or open workspace state) with explicit finish/
cancel/rehome guidance; approval rechecks those blockers inside the same
immediate Repo transaction as the cascade. Historical Jobs/runs, objectives, StockSage analysis/
queue rows, channel events/deliveries, artifact links, traces, and closed
workspace state remain separately governed unavailable provenance. The
inventory does not claim universal plugin discovery; unknown/plugin copies are
part of the disclosure. Canonical retention is otherwise unchanged and indefinite; there is
no automatic conversation pruning. Deletion removes only the canonical
Conversations rows and Search derivative. Non-FK Objectives, Jobs/runs, traces,
artifact links/content, and workspace canvas/ephemeral state survive as
separately governed data, retain at most an unavailable provenance reference,
and must tolerate a missing thread/message without restoring authority. The
confirmation discloses those surviving Allbert-managed classes as well as
independently kept Memory, channel/provider copies, backups, exports, snapshots,
and storage remnants.
Deletion is the operator-facing counterpart to Memory Forget: Forget removes
what Allbert concluded, while this removes what Allbert recorded as said. The
two are deliberately separate confirmed acts with separate consequences.

## E. Memory Flow

### E1. Collection and proposal production

```text
memory-consolidation managed job or on-demand registered action
  |
  +-- memory.consolidation.enabled=false -> recorded no-op, no source read
  +-- source-class grant absent           -> recorded no-op, guidance
  +-- pending count at cap                -> recorded no-op, review prompt
  v
bounded Corpus scan (default initial lookback 30 days)
  -> verified operator SourceEnvelopes only
  -> optional bounded same-conversation assistant context
       (transient disambiguation only; never evidence)
  -> deterministic segmentation/normalization/secret rejection
  -> configured local-model advisory extraction, if available
       (no hosted fallback; deterministic high-confidence subset or abstain)
  -> SPAN PROVENANCE GATE (LD 59)
       every semantic field of a consolidation-generated conversation proposal
       maps to an operator-authored source span as:
       source id + source digest + UTF-8 byte start/end + raw-span digest +
       normalized value + deterministic transform kind/version
       assistant context may resolve deixis only by selecting another
       operator-authored span; it can supply no field value
       uncovered field -> proposal REFUSED (counted, never accepted with
       assistant-derived content)
  -> model-independent identity, source, class, secret, digest, dedupe,
     conflict, and policy gates
  -> at most 20 new proposals/run; stop at 50 pending
  -> redacted run summary and next Corpus cursor
```

Traces and objective IDs may be retained as bounded correlation references
already attached to an originating conversation. Trace bodies, objective text,
job history, assistant claims, Search matches, and FTS snippets do not originate
or strengthen a v1.3 memory proposal.

The invariant is enforced by span coverage rather than by asserting the model
ignored text in its own prompt. Coordinates are UTF-8 byte offsets into the
content whose recorded digest is rechecked at review. The complete transform
allowlist is `identity_v1`, `trim_ascii_whitespace_v1`, `ascii_lower_v1`,
`operator_pronoun_v1` (`I | me | my` to the authenticated operator subject),
and `explicit_iso8601_date_v1` (an already explicit `YYYY-MM-DD` span). No
parser infers a synonym, date, number, or replacement text. A bounded assistant
excerpt may
be supplied for deixis resolution ("the second one", "there", "she"), but the
accepted claim's field values must be deterministically recoverable from the
operator-authored spans. If the value exists only in assistant text, extraction
abstains. This is what makes
`v13-assistant-not-authority-001` mechanically checkable: an opaque local model
cannot be proven to have discounted context, whereas span coverage is a
deterministic property of the stored proposal.

This span contract applies only to consolidation-generated proposals derived
from conversations. Explicit remember/update actions, confirmed manual
imports, and exact-confirmed legacy drafts do not fabricate conversation spans;
they enter the same claim writer with typed actor/action provenance appropriate
to their real authority path.

An ordinary durable proposal contains normalized proposed claim fields, typed
source IDs and current digests, classification, proposal revision/idempotency
key, extractor profile/version, run id, and review state. It does not copy a
transcript. Review fetches a bounded redacted excerpt from Corpus. A protected
third-party/minor/dependent proposal stores only a redacted pending stub before
individual review. Secret-filter hits are dropped and counted, never stored in
either form.

As implemented in M4, the deterministic protected gate runs on the current
verified operator source before bounded conversation context is fetched:
credential-shaped input and labeled financial identifiers are count-only hard
drops; clearly dependent or third-party private facts produce content-free
stubs; all other input continues to span-grounded ordinary extraction. A
canonical source deletion reduces exact matching active proposals and
source-bound legacy drafts to content-free stale outcomes after commit; replay
of the same approved deletion re-drives that cleanup. Grant loss is reconciled
before list DTOs are returned and during the existing managed Memory rebuild,
so a revoked proposal is not rendered from retained content.

The registered consolidation action uses additive `:memory_propose`. A current
verified collection grant authorizes only bounded writes of inert proposals,
so scheduled runs do not ask for confirmation every week. That permission
cannot keep/edit/archive/restore/Forget a claim. Those remain separately
