Quality gates accompany the behavioral rows: Memory extraction measures both
precision and recall plus abstention, temporal update/conflict, stale-source,
protected-data, and resurrection cases. Search measures deterministic
term/phrase/prefix/filter relevance, deletion/revocation precision, generation
freshness, scale/latency, and no raw-query leakage. Thresholds and reference
local-model identity were calibrated in M1 and are frozen; external projects are
research inputs, not moving runtime dependencies.

The frozen fixture/digest and numeric gates are plan LD 82. In short: eligible
proposal precision `>= 0.90`, recall `>= 0.80`, required abstention `>= 0.95`
over 20 rows (at most one miss),
temporal updates `4/4`, protected hard drops `2/2`, and protected individual-
review routing `2/2`, optionally advised by the local-only
`local_ollama/llama3.2:3b` profile. Active Memory retains an 8,000-byte prompt
budget, `top_k: 5`, and 2,048-byte chunks; at 10,000 current claims its warmed
single-caller selection/reauthorization is p95 `<= 75 ms`, p99 `<= 250 ms` over
200 queries. At 25,000 Search messages/at least 250 threads and 256 average
UTF-8 bytes, warmed query plus reauthorization is p95 `<= 200 ms`, p99
`<= 750 ms` over 300 mixed queries. The packaged macOS arm64 operator host and
packaged Linux x86_64 Serenity host report separately. Normal dirty ingestion
is visible within 90 seconds; hourly reconcile bounds a missed wakeup.

## K. External Smokes And Focused Operator Validation

All validation uses a disposable Allbert Home and an exact clean pushed SHA.
Product acceptance uses real configured providers/channels; fakes and fixtures
remain automated-test tools. After v0.55.1, interactive validation uses one warm
TUI session unless a row explicitly tests startup or failure.

There are three deliberately different, serial TUI paths:

- **Focused adaptive-fan-out qualification — required before the full source
  walk-through:** after M9.b.4.3/M9.b.5.3's focused automated rejoin and the
  implementing agent's exact real-provider rehearsal, the operator runs only
  FOV-0/FOV-1/FOV-2, the repaired FOV-4 behavior, FOV-7R inspection, and FOV-8
  cleanup on a different disposable Home. It proves the repaired durable budget,
  worker, and complete semantic fan-in path. Supplied-data, counted-protocol,
  and ordinary-response non-regression are not repeated here; the complete
  source SV row owns them next. Focused FOV is not the first durable integration
  test, is not Memory/Search acceptance, and is never inherited as an `SV-*`
  PASS.

- **Source behavioral validation — required before candidate construction:** at
  one clean pushed SHA, run `mix allbert serve`, attach with `mix allbert tui`,
  and exercise K2/K3/K4 through the unified `mix allbert ...` dispatcher. This
  is the primary operator-UX gate. No package rebuild or aggregate gate precedes
  it.
- **Exact-artifact parity — required before publication:** after source
  validation, construct the candidate and repeat attach/status/health, both
  routing-boundary prompts, and one representative Memory, Search, and managed-
  job read through the packaged `allbert`. The package row proves byte/install
  parity; it does not repeat the full source lifecycle without evidence of a
  package-only divergence. Packaged FV remains release acceptance.

All core single-turn model calls preserve one message-provenance invariant.
Allbert-authored, declarative instructions occupy the system role. Reviewed
Memory, retrieved context, candidate lists, and registered/runtime metadata are
explicitly inert user-role reference or advisory data. The typed task input is
the final user message; DirectAnswer uses the current operator input there, and
vision parts attach only to it. These roles shape model behavior; none grants
action authority or replaces Registry, Runner, Security Central, confirmations,
or the registered Jido action boundary.

DirectAnswer also has a distinct qualification boundary. The global
`local`/curated first-model profile remains `local_ollama/llama3.2:3b`; it
proves the modest consumer substrate, not DirectAnswer semantic fidelity. The
shipped DirectAnswer task head is
`direct_answer_local` (`local_ollama/qwen2.5:7b`, temperature `0`, maximum
`1024` output tokens, 60-second timeout). A non-empty DirectAnswer task list is
closed and authoritative in operator-authored order; it never gains the global
primary or an unrelated hosted provider implicitly. An empty list alone retains
primary compatibility fallback. The operator may explicitly select a hosted
task head, but pre-egress disclosure remains mandatory.

Selection and transport share one bounded route projection. The registered
purpose-specific action moves a profile to the DirectAnswer head, preserves and
deduplicates the authored fallback tail, enables that provider, audits the
writes, and leaves global primary unchanged. Disclosure records the ordered
primary plus at most one callable fallback. Any route-set change invalidates
all Web/TUI/CLI acknowledgements; TUI renders before attach, Web refreshes in
the same LiveView, and provider admission rechecks the exact current set before
each hosted ordinary-text call. A mapped DM or other non-presenting channel can
reuse only an exact local operator-control acknowledgement in the same Home.
That record permits configured provider egress, not channel access or
cross-surface history.

Do not run two validation daemons concurrently or against one Home. If packaged
`allbert` fails after source validation, classify the issue as package/install
parity and repeat only the affected packaged row after remediation. If source
validation fails, classify the finding before any candidate is built: repair
environment/documentation locally, or batch executable findings into one
bounded tranche with focused owner tests. When executable inputs change in the
pushed SHA, close the old session, restart SV-0 through SV-5 on a fresh Home,
then repeat only the affected/dependent behavioral rows. A docs-only commit uses
diff hygiene and the docs gate without invalidating accepted runtime rows. A
source PASS authorizes candidate construction; exact-artifact parity is still
required before publication.

The v1.3 execution order is the focused `FOV-*` adaptive-fan-out row, a fresh
full source setup plus K2/K3/readable K4 and source operator validation, K5
complete local candidate construction, K6 packaged latency on
macOS arm64 and Serenity Linux x64, short exact-artifact parity on macOS,
remaining automated/available-channel rows, the one final aggregate rejoin, and
protected promotion.

The human operator validates ordinary product behavior, not fault injection or
internal storage choreography. K2 and K3 are the primary attended walk-through;
the readable job observations in K4 may be performed in the same session.
Focused behavioral suites and artifact capability rows retain restart-at-phase,
busy-WAL, stale-cursor, collision, purge-interruption, and every-generation
byte-scan proofs. This split does not waive those rows; it prevents a human
runbook from becoming a second test framework.

Both attended source rows are self-contained below. Run `FOV-*` first; after it
passes and closes its Home, start the complete v1.3 row at SV-0 from the source
checkout and a new Home. Run each numbered step separately and wait for the
`bash-3.2$` prompt before starting the next one. When a numbered step contains a
multi-line shell block, paste that whole fenced block; transient `>` continuation
prompts are normal while Bash receives a loop or conditional. If Bash still shows
`>` after the complete fenced block was pasted, press Ctrl-C once and restart only
that numbered step. Never paste a later numbered step into an unfinished command.
TUI input is different: every attended prompt below is one physical line because
Enter submits the turn. Run only one provider/model call at a time and wait for
its `allbert>` prompt before starting the next call.

The focused shell intentionally uses `set -Euo pipefail` without interactive
`errexit`. Every machine PASS is owned by an explicit conditional. This keeps a
failed row, its daemon, its variables, and its evidence available for inspection
and FOV-8 cleanup, while preventing queued paste input from falling through to a
parent zsh and printing a false PASS after Bash exits.

The source row binds `HEAD` directly to `origin/main`; the provisional `v1.3.0`
tag may still name a rejected candidate and is deliberately not consulted.
SV-1 creates a new private operator root, and terminal 2 imports the exact values
from one mode-0600 file. Only Allbert-generated proposal, claim, thread,
confirmation, and job ids are derived from command output later.

### K-fanout. Focused manager/worker/composer operator qualification

**COMPLETED AND ARCHIVED, 2026-08-02.** FOV-0 through FOV-8 were run
attended and all passed. Everything below is the record of that run, kept
for provenance. Its three extracted scripts now live in
`docs/plans/archives/v1.3-fov-scripts/` and are no longer executed by any
test, because they are v1.3-scoped and release-specific validation scripts
are not permanent repository fixtures. They are not verified against the
current schema, renderer, or CLI; do not treat these blocks as a runbook
for a later release. The live attended runbook for v1.3 closeout is the
SV-0 through SV-9 sequence.

This is operator confirmation after M9.b.6, not a debugging or first-
integration step. It uses ordinary source commands and a real configured local
model. The literal blocks below are reconciled to executable commit `dc1d4647`
and the single-call receipt/selection contracts. The configured-provider matrix
is GREEN on the shipped default at that SHA. These commands become an operator
instruction only after the agent rehearsal passes and an independent review is
clear; they are not themselves a claimed PASS.
Automated focused tests—not this human row—own persistence corruption, provider
failure, deterministic fallback, partial failure, maximum report bounds, race,
restart, receipt, budget, cancellation/parent death, and duplicate-signal
injection.

Two attended findings explain the current hold. The first FOV-4 created no
Objective and reopened manager admission; the `1b4d3014`/`27b974a1` tranche
repaired that boundary. The next FOV-4 did create two completed durable children
and one delivered parent, but the parent selected
`deterministic_fallback/budget_denied`: generic persistence redaction had changed
the two numeric output-token budget fields into redaction markers before
rehydration, so the healthy composer was never called. The fallback then reused
two child observations silently cut at 500 bytes and ended them mid-word even
though the child Objective rows retained complete results. That run fails
FOV-4; do not continue it as acceptance.

M9.b.4.1/M9.b.5.1 repaired those structural defects and the implementing agent
then ran this exact flow from clean pushed SHA `0777f1a0`. The manager planned
and admitted two children, both completed, one 3,934-byte central report selected
the healthy `model` path and complementary `[0,1]` layout, delivery completed,
both databases passed integrity, and stopped-daemon logging contained no debug
query or warning/error/crash header. FOV-4 still failed: a child incorrectly
described `rest_for_one`, a second child implied exactly-once replay, and the
generic layout prose never supplied the requested substantive complement. This
is the RED evidence for M9.b.4.2/M9.b.5.2. Healthy infrastructure and closed
metadata are necessary but cannot substitute for semantic product behavior.

Before these numbered operator commands become active, Build Progress must
record the following evidence on the clean pushed SHA named for each completed
slice; any later executable change must converge its edits and a fresh
agent rehearsal on one final clean pushed SHA. The executable/focused/static/
audit portions are complete at `dc1d4647`, and the configured-provider matrix is
GREEN on the shipped default at that SHA. The agent rehearsal is the remaining
pre-operator barrier:

1. Generic token-key redaction remains strict, while the closed fan-out
   provenance codec round-trips the parent and proposal-event budgets as typed
   integers and rejects malformed, extra, redacted, negative, or string-coerced
   values.
2. Public Runtime/Repo tests cross `frame -> persist -> reload -> terminalize ->
   composer authorization -> report selection -> delivery`; the healthy row
   enters one composer lifecycle, records its exact three/six provider-call
   phases, and stores source `model`, while corrupt budget enters it zero times
   and records an honest fallback.
3. Separate two-, three-, and maximum-child report tests prove complete
   under-bound evidence, deterministic order/status/receipts, valid UTF-8,
   explicit readable shortening only when required, retrieval guidance for full
   child truth, and the final body bound.
4. The one opt-in mixed configured-provider comparison is complete bounded
   performance RED at `3bb42119`. Freeze its evidence and return to the operator
   without changing defaults or starting FOV.
5. The literal commands and expectations below are reconciled against that
   rehearsal, docs/static checks pass, and an independent drift audit is clear.

The prefilter is itself qualified before use: manager/composer and worker-
quality fixtures are schema-checked and canonical-digest-bound before Home
creation/migration; one public orchestrator emits both phase rows and propagates
either phase failure; and focused fakes prove default/explicit fixture routing
without provider access. One monotonic row deadline covers generation;
every provider invocation receives only the remaining time. Current metrics
retain only closed phase/group/call aggregates, revision use, per-row allowlisted
failure stage/reason and failed-rule count, four resolved role bindings with
profile/provider/model/effective-endpoint and configuration digests, corpus
provenance, fixture digests, and already-authorized report digests. Raw prompts,
drafts, answers, source-handle contents, provider output, arbitrary nested
errors, and failed-rule ids
remain excluded. This is a protocol prefilter; semantic acceptance still
requires the attended clean-SHA FOV below.

6. One declarative, task-neutral child-quality contract composes the existing
   DirectAnswer rules with only fan-out task-coverage rules and derives
   generation, automated-scenario, and attended criteria from the original

   Every enclosing Jido command and ReqLLM request disables retries; a retryable
   framework/transport failure is one attempted provider request and one honest

   call instead of being rebound to the new protocol.

   v1, the existing
   `run_completed` transaction writes the exact transient Step id to
   `Objective.current_step_id`, finalizes that Step, persists its correlated
   event, and performs any final parent layout-v2 freeze atomically. Report code
   verifies rather than reconstructs that binding, and layout-v2 input binds the

   or FOV-specific fact is production policy.
   Verified operator steering replaces stale compiled expected-result guidance
   with the fixed task-neutral instruction `Complete the operator-steered child
   task.` and binds the directive/event digests into the same task contract. A
   reviewed child's `run_completed` payload contains exactly the bounded,
   content-free typed quality receipt plus the existing atomic
   `step_id`/`step_status` correlation:
   its exact normalized answer remains on the Objective/Step and in the post-
   commit signal, avoiding duplicate prose at the existing 2,000-character

7. ADR 0021/0083 and the central report contract permit a bounded model-authored
   advisory synthesis while Allbert still deterministically owns status,
   failure/cancellation, receipts, ordered authoritative child observations,
   bounds, digests, durable selection, and delivery. A private Jido synthesis

   and revisable, while local schema validation continues to require one exact

   The 16-child maximum encoded `fanout_report_selected` event, including all

8. Focused fake scenario matrices cover every draft/critique/revision/

#### FOV-0 — create one clean focused source session

Open terminal 1, run this command by itself, and wait for `bash-3.2$`:

```sh
bash --noprofile --norc
```

At the `bash-3.2$` prompt, bind the literal source checkout before creating any
Home, settings, database, or daemon:

```sh
cd /Users/spuri/projects/lexlapax/allbert-assist || exit 1
```

Wait for `bash-3.2$`, then paste this complete block once:

```sh
set -Euo pipefail
umask 077
FOV_DAEMON_PID=
FOV_DAEMON_LOG=
FOV_ROOT=
FOV_SOURCE_SHA=
trap 'if test -n "${FOV_DAEMON_PID:-}"; then kill "$FOV_DAEMON_PID" 2>/dev/null || true; wait "$FOV_DAEMON_PID" 2>/dev/null || true; fi' EXIT
export MIX_ENV=dev

if (exec 3<>/dev/tcp/127.0.0.1/4137) 2>/dev/null; then
  echo 'FAIL FOV-0: port 4137 is occupied'
elif git fetch --quiet origin main &&
     FOV_SOURCE_SHA="$(git rev-parse HEAD)" &&
     test "$FOV_SOURCE_SHA" = "$(git rev-parse origin/main)" &&
     test -z "$(git status --porcelain)" &&
     mix compile --warnings-as-errors &&
     FOV_ROOT="$(mktemp -d "${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/allbert-v13-fanout.XXXXXXXX")" &&
     mkdir -m 700 "$FOV_ROOT/home" &&
     printf '%s\n' "$FOV_SOURCE_SHA" > "$FOV_ROOT/source-sha.txt" &&
     openssl rand -base64 32 > "$FOV_ROOT/settings-master-key" &&
     chmod 600 "$FOV_ROOT/settings-master-key"
then
  if ALLBERT_HOME="$FOV_ROOT/home" &&
     ALLBERT_SETTINGS_MASTER_KEY="$(tr -d '\r\n' < "$FOV_ROOT/settings-master-key")" &&
     export ALLBERT_HOME ALLBERT_SETTINGS_MASTER_KEY &&
     mkdir -m 700 "$ALLBERT_HOME/log" &&
     FOV_DAEMON_LOG="$ALLBERT_HOME/log/source-daemon.log" &&
     mix allbert.ecto.migrate --quiet
  then
    PORT=4137 mix allbert serve > "$FOV_DAEMON_LOG" 2>&1 &
    FOV_DAEMON_PID=$!
    printf 'FOV-0 source SHA: %s\n' "$FOV_SOURCE_SHA"
    printf 'FOV-0 daemon PID: %s\n' "$FOV_DAEMON_PID"
    printf 'FOV-0 evidence root: %s\n' "$FOV_ROOT"
    printf 'FOV-0 daemon log: %s\n' "$FOV_DAEMON_LOG"
    FOV_READY=false

    for ((attempt=1; attempt<=90; attempt++)); do
      if curl -fsS --max-time 2 http://127.0.0.1:4137/health 2>/dev/null | grep -q '"status":"ok"'; then
        FOV_READY=true
        break
      fi
      if ! kill -0 "$FOV_DAEMON_PID" 2>/dev/null; then
        break
      fi
      sleep 1
    done

    if test "$FOV_READY" = true; then
      echo 'PASS FOV-0: clean focused Home and source daemon are ready'
    else
      tail -n 80 "$FOV_DAEMON_LOG" >&2 || true
      echo 'FAIL FOV-0: source daemon did not become healthy within 90 seconds'
    fi
  else
    echo "FOV-0 evidence root: $FOV_ROOT"
    echo 'FAIL FOV-0: migration did not complete'
  fi
else
  if test -n "$FOV_ROOT"; then
    echo "FOV-0 evidence root: $FOV_ROOT"
  fi
  echo 'FAIL FOV-0: pushed-source, compile, or private-Home preparation failed'
fi
```

Expected output shape; the named line contains the literal numeric PID. Compile,
migration, or background-job output may precede it:

```text
FOV-0 source SHA: <the 40-character clean pushed SHA in FOV_SOURCE_SHA>
FOV-0 daemon PID: <the positive integer stored in FOV_DAEMON_PID>
FOV-0 evidence root: <the private temporary directory stored in FOV_ROOT>
FOV-0 daemon log: <FOV_ROOT>/home/log/source-daemon.log
PASS FOV-0: clean focused Home and source daemon are ready
```

Record the printed integer. FOV-2 `/status` must show that exact value as
`beam_os_pid`; do not infer it from the shell's background-job decoration.

(2) Analyze how an append-only event log plus a rebuildable projection improves crash recovery, including idempotency and replay. the prompt while they run. Wait for exactly one joined report. The central
report must contain these exact structural/semantic outcomes:

The Jido worker and composer lifecycles are implementation detail. They must not

must therefore be complete. A mid-sentence/mid-word ending, an unannounced fixed

**Content is scored as parity, not absolute correctness (M9.b.6).** The rows
above are FOV-4a: orchestration, structure, receipts, and bounds, and they are
absolute. Content is FOV-4b and is scored against the frozen single-turn control
recorded before implementation — the same prompt answered by one DirectAnswer
call on the same configured head with no manager, children, or synthesis. Each
child observation passes when it is not materially worse than the control on the

control nor its sibling, and is complete within bounds. A child that is wrong in

restart strategies or event-log semantics is qualified in the DirectAnswer
task-head row, not here, because no fan-out topology can add knowledge the
configured model lacks. Five model families demonstrated exactly that.

There is no `Attention required (not model-arranged):` section and no failed,

child. Each complete stored child observation appears exactly once in the

mutate either observation.

```sh
trap 'if test -n "${V13_TUI_DAEMON_PID:-}"; then kill "$V13_TUI_DAEMON_PID" 2>/dev/null || true; wait "$V13_TUI_DAEMON_PID" 2>/dev/null || true; fi' EXIT
test -n "${BASH_VERSION:-}" || { echo 'FAIL SV-0: not running under the bash started on the first line; the block did not paste as one unit'; false; }
case "$-" in *e*) : ;; *) echo 'FAIL SV-0: errexit is not set; re-run this block'; false ;; esac
case "$(trap -p EXIT)" in *V13_TUI_DAEMON_PID*) : ;; *) echo 'FAIL SV-0: the daemon cleanup trap is not installed'; false ;; esac
git fetch --quiet origin main
test "$(git rev-parse HEAD)" = "$(git rev-parse origin/main)"
test -z "$(git status --porcelain)"
export MIX_ENV=dev
if (exec 3<>/dev/tcp/127.0.0.1/4137) 2>/dev/null; then echo 'FAIL SV-0: port 4137 is occupied'; exit 1; fi
mix compile --force --warnings-as-errors
echo 'PASS SV-0: clean pushed source and port 4137 are ready'
```

Expected final output, literally:

```text
PASS SV-0: clean pushed source and port 4137 are ready
```

The prompt must read `bash-3.2$` for every line of this block. If it reverts
to your login-shell prompt partway through, the block did not run as one unit:
a lost newline made one line swallow the next, the failed command tripped
`errexit`, and the bash session exited. Everything after that point ran in your
login shell with no `errexit`, no `umask 077`, and — the part that matters — no
EXIT trap to stop the daemon. The three guard lines above exist to catch that;
on any `FAIL SV-0` line, press `Ctrl-D` until you are back at your login shell
and start SV-0 again, pasting the block in smaller pieces. A clean compile does
not redeem a failed guard: SV-0's purpose is the shell contract, not the build.

#### SV-1 — create and migrate a fresh disposable Home

> **v1.3 M9.b.10.d.** `${V13_TMPDIR%/}` strips the trailing slash on purpose.
> macOS sets `TMPDIR` with one, so a bare `mktemp -d "${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/..."`
> yields a doubled separator (`T//allbert-…`). Allbert normalizes the paths it
> prints, so every later literal path comparison — `grep -Fq "$V13_MEMORY_PATH"`
> in SV-6C above all — compares a doubled-slash shell variable against
> single-slash output, fails, and ends the session under `errexit`. This bit an
> attended run on 2026-08-03. Strip the slash rather than resolving with
> `pwd -P` or `realpath`: those also rewrite `/var` to `/private/var` on macOS,
> which reintroduces the same mismatch in the other direction. Normalizing once
> here fixes the whole class rather than each comparison.

Run this block once:

```sh
V13_TMPDIR="${TMPDIR:-/tmp}"
V13_OPERATOR_ROOT="$(mktemp -d "${V13_TMPDIR%/}/allbert-v13-source.XXXXXXXX")"
mkdir -m 700 "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/home"
openssl rand -base64 32 > "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/settings-master-key"
chmod 600 "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/settings-master-key"
export ALLBERT_HOME="$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/home"
export ALLBERT_SETTINGS_MASTER_KEY="$(tr -d '\r\n' < "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/settings-master-key")"
export MIX_ENV=dev
export V13_TUI_PORT=4137
printf 'export V13_OPERATOR_ROOT=%q\nexport ALLBERT_HOME=%q\nexport MIX_ENV=dev\nexport V13_TUI_PORT=4137\n' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT" "$ALLBERT_HOME" > /tmp/allbert-v13-source-validation.env
chmod 600 /tmp/allbert-v13-source-validation.env
mix allbert.ecto.migrate --quiet
echo 'PASS SV-1: fresh source-validation Home is migrated'
```

Expected final output, literally:

```text
PASS SV-1: fresh source-validation Home is migrated
```

#### SV-2 — start the source daemon and prove readiness

```sh
PORT=4137 mix allbert serve > "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/daemon.log" 2>&1 &
V13_TUI_DAEMON_PID=$!
printf 'export V13_TUI_DAEMON_PID=%q\n' "$V13_TUI_DAEMON_PID" >> /tmp/allbert-v13-source-validation.env
V13_DAEMON_READY=false
for ((attempt=1; attempt<=90; attempt++)); do if curl -fsS --max-time 2 http://127.0.0.1:4137/health 2>/dev/null | grep -q '"status":"ok"'; then V13_DAEMON_READY=true; break; fi; if ! kill -0 "$V13_TUI_DAEMON_PID" 2>/dev/null; then tail -n 80 "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/daemon.log"; exit 1; fi; sleep 1; done
test "$V13_DAEMON_READY" = true
echo "PASS SV-2: source daemon $V13_TUI_DAEMON_PID is healthy"
```

The final line begins `PASS SV-2: source daemon ` and ends ` is healthy`; the
integer in between is the daemon PID used again in SV-5. Starting the one
source daemon before runtime-backed setup keeps the remaining commands on the
same attach/API path instead of cold-booting Allbert for every setting write.

Terminal 1 now owns the daemon's lifetime. Its EXIT trap stops the daemon
whenever the bash session ends — including when `errexit` ends it after a failed
command. If terminal 1 dies, the daemon dies, and `/tmp/allbert-v13-source-validation.env` deliberately does not carry
`ALLBERT_SETTINGS_MASTER_KEY`, so a new shell cannot simply source it and
continue. To recover, start a fresh terminal 1 with the SV-0 block, then:

```sh
. /tmp/allbert-v13-source-validation.env
export ALLBERT_SETTINGS_MASTER_KEY="$(tr -d '\r\n' < "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/settings-master-key")"
test -d "$ALLBERT_HOME"
```

and re-run SV-2 to start a new daemon. The Home and every setting written into
it survive; only the process is gone. Whether to resume there or restart the
whole row on a fresh Home is a judgment call about the evidence record: resume
if nothing wrote unintended state, restart if the failure left stray pending
confirmations, partial writes, or anything else a later step could trip over.

#### SV-3 — configure and preflight the real provider through the daemon

Run each command separately:

```sh
curl -fsS --max-time 3 http://127.0.0.1:4137/health | grep -q '"status":"ok"' || { echo 'FAIL SV-3: the SV-2 source daemon is not healthy; SV-3 must run through it, not cold-boot'; false; }
mix allbert admin models use-direct-answer direct_answer_local | tee "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/direct-answer-selection.txt"
mix allbert admin settings set intent.model_assist_enabled true
mix allbert admin settings set intent.direct_answer_model_enabled true
mix allbert admin models doctor local | tee "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/doctor-local.txt"
mix allbert admin models doctor direct_answer_local | tee "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/doctor-direct-answer.txt"
grep -q '^Model profile local: provider=local_ollama, model=llama3.2:3b\.' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/doctor-local.txt"
grep -q '^endpoint_ok=true$' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/doctor-local.txt"
grep -q '^model_available=true$' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/doctor-local.txt"
grep -q '^Model profile direct_answer_local: provider=local_ollama, model=qwen2.5:7b\.' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/doctor-direct-answer.txt"
grep -q '^endpoint_ok=true$' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/doctor-direct-answer.txt"
grep -q '^model_available=true$' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/doctor-direct-answer.txt"
grep -q '^DirectAnswer profile set to direct_answer_local; provider local_ollama enabled; global primary unchanged\.$' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/direct-answer-selection.txt"
echo 'PASS SV-3: global local and qualified DirectAnswer profiles are available through the source daemon'
```

Required literal lines include:

```text
Model profile local: provider=local_ollama, model=llama3.2:3b.
Model profile direct_answer_local: provider=local_ollama, model=qwen2.5:7b.
DirectAnswer profile set to direct_answer_local; provider local_ollama enabled; global primary unchanged.
endpoint_ok=true
model_available=true
PASS SV-3: global local and qualified DirectAnswer profiles are available through the source daemon
```

Each doctor must print `endpoint_ok=true` and `model_available=true`. The
purpose-specific command enables `local_ollama`, selects only the
DirectAnswer task head, and leaves the default global `local` primary
unchanged. It does not introduce `local` into the DirectAnswer fallback tail.

If the local doctor passes but the DirectAnswer doctor reports
`model_available=false`, print `SV-3 BLOCKED_PROVIDER: qwen2.5:7b is not
pulled`. Use the web Models catalog's `direct_answer` row to review and confirm
the Qwen pull, or run this literal confirmation-gated repair in terminal 2:

```sh
V13_QWEN_PULL_OUTPUT="$(mix allbert admin model pull --model qwen2.5:7b 2>&1 || true)"
printf '%s\n' "$V13_QWEN_PULL_OUTPUT"
V13_QWEN_PULL_ID="$(printf '%s\n' "$V13_QWEN_PULL_OUTPUT" | sed -n 's/.*Confirmation request: \(conf_[0-9][0-9_]*\).*/\1/p' | tail -n 1)"
test -n "$V13_QWEN_PULL_ID"
mix allbert admin confirmations approve "$V13_QWEN_PULL_ID" --reason 'pull qualified DirectAnswer model'
mix allbert admin models doctor direct_answer_local | tee "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/doctor-direct-answer.txt"
grep -q '^endpoint_ok=true$' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/doctor-direct-answer.txt"
grep -q '^model_available=true$' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/doctor-direct-answer.txt"
echo 'PASS SV-3-REPAIR: qwen2.5:7b was explicitly approved and is available'
```

Run the repair **only** when the DirectAnswer doctor reported
`model_available=false`. If it already reported `true`, the model is present and
this block does nothing but create a stray pending confirmation in the Home.

Run it in terminal 2, never in terminal 1. `model pull` exits non-zero by design
whenever it creates a confirmation — it raises `Model pull is ready for approval
... Nothing was pulled.` — so under terminal 1's `errexit` it would end the bash
session, and terminal 1's EXIT trap would stop the source daemon with it. The
`|| true` above keeps the capture from tripping `errexit`; the `test -n
"$V13_QWEN_PULL_ID"` line below is what fails the row if the pull genuinely did
not produce a confirmation.

The repair downloads the external Ollama model and may take time. It never runs
silently. After its literal PASS, rerun the main SV-3 block so the complete row
is captured. Stop with `SV-3 BLOCKED_PROVIDER` for an unreachable endpoint,
failed pull, or any remaining false value; a fake or canned provider cannot
satisfy operator acceptance.

#### SV-4 — attach the source TUI and prove baseline behavior

Run in terminal 1:

```sh
ERL_AFLAGS="${ERL_AFLAGS:+$ERL_AFLAGS }+Bc" mix allbert tui
```

Expect these four lines, in this order. This is output to read, not text to
enter:

```text
Your configured DirectAnswer route uses direct_answer_local from local_ollama. Inference uses your configured local endpoint. Change the model in Models, or disable model answers with `allbert admin settings set intent.direct_answer_model_enabled false`.
Your configured fan-out manager route uses direct_answer_local from local_ollama. Inference uses your configured local endpoint. Change `model_preferences.tasks.fanout_manager`, or disable model answers with `allbert admin settings set intent.direct_answer_model_enabled false`.
Your configured fan-out report synthesis route uses direct_answer_local from local_ollama. Inference uses your configured local endpoint. Change `model_preferences.tasks.fanout_synthesis`, or disable model answers with `allbert admin settings set intent.direct_answer_model_enabled false`.
Allbert TUI - daemon attached
```

All three disclosures must be emitted before the attach line. Together they
acknowledge only the exact configured route set that was rendered; if terminal
output fails, the TUI must not attach.

**Do not judge this from the terminal.** The TUI takes over the screen as it
attaches (`Cli.Tui.Client.render_initial/1` writes the attach line through
`write_static` once the client owns the terminal), so the disclosure is
routinely scrolled away or cleared before you can read it. Seeing only
`Allbert TUI - daemon attached` is expected and is **not** a failure. Verify it
where it is durably recorded instead, in terminal 2:

```sh
python3 -c "
import json,os
d=json.load(open(os.path.join(os.environ['ALLBERT_HOME'],'onboarding.json')))
s=d.get('model_disclosure',{}).get('tui',{})
usages=s.get('usages',[])
assert s.get('state') == 'acknowledged'
assert s.get('profile') == 'direct_answer_local'
assert usages == ['primary','fanout_manager','fanout_synthesis']
print('tui disclosure state =', s.get('state'), '| profile =', s.get('profile'), '| usages =', ','.join(usages))
"
```

Expected output, literally:

```text
tui disclosure state = acknowledged | profile = direct_answer_local | usages = primary,fanout_manager,fanout_synthesis
```

PASS: `state = acknowledged` and `profile = direct_answer_local`. A `pending`
state after a first attach is the real failure this row is looking for.

At the `allbert>` prompt, enter these three lines one at a time:

```text
/status
what is 2+2?
/health
```

Required observations are `channel: tui`, a real-provider answer equivalent to
`4`, and `Health: ok.`. Report `PASS SV-4` after all three observations. Keep
this TUI open through SV-9.

#### PV-1 — bind the exact installed candidate

Still in terminal 1, run:

```sh
V13_CANDIDATE_DIR='__REPLACE_ONCE_WITH_QUALIFIED_CANDIDATE_DIRECTORY__'
case "$V13_CANDIDATE_DIR" in __*) echo 'FAIL PV-1: replace the candidate-directory token first'; exit 1 ;; esac
V13_CANDIDATE_DIR="$(cd -P -- "$V13_CANDIDATE_DIR" && pwd -P)"
V13_CANDIDATE_ARCHIVE="$V13_CANDIDATE_DIR/allbert-v1.3.0-macos-arm64.tar.gz"

V13_CANDIDATE_SMOKE="$V13_CANDIDATE_DIR/smoke-macos-arm64.json"
test -f "$V13_CANDIDATE_ARCHIVE"
test -f "$V13_CANDIDATE_TOOLCHAIN"
test -f "$V13_CANDIDATE_SMOKE"
V13_CANDIDATE_SHA="$(jq -er '.source_sha | select(test("^[0-9a-f]{40}$"))' "$V13_CANDIDATE_TOOLCHAIN")"
V13_CANDIDATE_GENERATION="$(jq -er '.generation | select(length > 0)' "$V13_CANDIDATE_TOOLCHAIN")"
V13_CANDIDATE_ARCHIVE_SHA="$(shasum -a 256 "$V13_CANDIDATE_ARCHIVE" | awk '{print $1}')"
jq -e --arg sha "$V13_CANDIDATE_SHA" --arg generation "$V13_CANDIDATE_GENERATION" --arg digest "$V13_CANDIDATE_ARCHIVE_SHA" '.target == "macos-arm64" and .source_sha == $sha and .generation == $generation and .archive_sha256 == $digest and .outcome == "passed"' "$V13_CANDIDATE_SMOKE" >/dev/null
git merge-base --is-ancestor "$V13_CANDIDATE_SHA" origin/main
test -x /opt/homebrew/opt/allbert/bin/allbert
test "$(/opt/homebrew/opt/allbert/bin/allbert version)" = 'allbert 1.3.0'
test "$(brew info --json=v2 allbert-v13/candidate/allbert | jq -r '.formulae[0].installed[-1].version')" = '1.3.0'
test "$(brew info --json=v2 allbert-v13/candidate/allbert | jq -r '.formulae[0].full_name')" = 'allbert-v13/candidate/allbert'
test "$(brew info --json=v2 allbert-v13/candidate/allbert | jq -r '.formulae[0].urls.stable.checksum')" = "$V13_CANDIDATE_ARCHIVE_SHA"
echo "PASS PV-1: installed allbert 1.3.0 binds sha=$V13_CANDIDATE_SHA generation=$V13_CANDIDATE_GENERATION archive_sha256=$V13_CANDIDATE_ARCHIVE_SHA"
```

```text
PASS PV-1: installed allbert 1.3.0 binds sha=[40 lowercase hex] generation=[qualified generation] archive_sha256=[64 lowercase hex]
```

```text
Your configured DirectAnswer route uses direct_answer_local from local_ollama. Inference uses your configured local endpoint. Change the model in Models, or disable model answers with `allbert admin settings set intent.direct_answer_model_enabled false`.
Your configured fan-out manager route uses direct_answer_local from local_ollama. Inference uses your configured local endpoint. Change `model_preferences.tasks.fanout_manager`, or disable model answers with `allbert admin settings set intent.direct_answer_model_enabled false`.
Your configured fan-out report synthesis route uses direct_answer_local from local_ollama. Inference uses your configured local endpoint. Change `model_preferences.tasks.fanout_synthesis`, or disable model answers with `allbert admin settings set intent.direct_answer_model_enabled false`.
Allbert TUI - daemon attached
```

The packaged client must render the same exact route-derived disclosures before
it attaches. A missing, post-attach, extra, differently routed, or differently
ordered disclosure fails PV-5. As with SV-4, the alternate screen can hide the
primary-buffer text; PV-6 therefore verifies the durable acknowledged route set
instead of relying only on what remains visible after attach.

At the `allbert>` prompt, enter these three lines one at a time:

```text
/status
what is 2+2?
/health
```

Required observations are `channel: tui`, a real provider answer equivalent to
`4`, and `Health: ok.`. Do not type `/what is 2+2?`; a leading slash denotes a
TUI command. Keep this TUI open at the `allbert>` prompt for PV-7.
Report `PASS PV-5` after all three observations.

#### PV-6 — bind terminal 2 to the same packaged daemon and Home

Open terminal 2 at any directory and run this block once:

```sh
bash --noprofile --norc
set -Eeuo pipefail
umask 077
source /tmp/allbert-v13-validation-current.env
export ALLBERT_SETTINGS_MASTER_KEY="$(tr -d '\r\n' < "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/settings-master-key")"
export PATH="/opt/homebrew/opt/allbert/bin:$PATH"
test "$(command -v allbert)" = /opt/homebrew/opt/allbert/bin/allbert
allbert version
allbert admin health | tee "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/health.txt"
allbert admin status | tee "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/status.txt"
python3 -c "
import json,os
d=json.load(open(os.path.join(os.environ['ALLBERT_HOME'],'onboarding.json')))
s=d.get('model_disclosure',{}).get('tui',{})
usages=s.get('usages',[])
assert s.get('state') == 'acknowledged'
assert s.get('profile') == 'direct_answer_local'
assert usages == ['primary','fanout_manager','fanout_synthesis']
print('tui disclosure state =', s.get('state'), '| profile =', s.get('profile'), '| usages =', ','.join(usages))
"
grep -q '^Health: ok\.' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/health.txt"
grep -q "^- beam_os_pid: $V13_TUI_DAEMON_PID$" "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/status.txt"
grep -q '^- channel: cli$' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/status.txt"
echo "PASS PV-6: terminal 2 is bound to packaged daemon $V13_TUI_DAEMON_PID and the shared Home"
```

Required output includes the literal version and channel lines below. The health
line begins with the literal prefix shown and then includes service posture;
status also prints process and identity detail:

```text
allbert 1.3.0
Health: ok. service_platform=
- channel: cli
tui disclosure state = acknowledged | profile = direct_answer_local | usages = primary,fanout_manager,fanout_synthesis
```

The final PASS line begins
`PASS PV-6: terminal 2 is bound to packaged daemon `, continues with the
integer printed by PV-4, and ends ` and the shared Home`. The command itself
proves that the
`beam_os_pid` equals that PID; PV-5's TUI `/status` must show it too. This proves
both surfaces reached the same daemon. `channel: cli` is the expected surface
identity for an administrative command issued from terminal 2. If a check
fails under `set -e`, Bash exits to the parent shell; a later PASS line pasted
at the parent prompt does not count.

Keep only redacted outcomes in release evidence. Do not copy raw Memory values,
queries, snippets, credentials, keys, provider payloads, or generated ids into
the release-evidence summary.

Ollama `llama3.2:3b` and the distinct DirectAnswer profile

The packaged `linux-arm64` target runs capability/package smoke in the pinned

#### Package-only remediation cadence

SV-0 through SV-9 are the only source behavioral run. If a later PV row fails,
do not create another detached source harness: compare the affected PV outcome

and repeat only that PV row on a complete replacement generation. A package
FAIL never converts to PASS from source evidence alone.

### K0. v1.2.6 M0.b3 thin-client barrier

The named bounded runner is
`scripts/smoke/tui_qualification.sh`; its standard-library PTY helper keeps
bounded terminal bytes in memory only for assertions, discards daemon output,
and emits content-free PASS rows. It is a focused external smoke, not an aggregate Mix
gate. Run its source row against a disposable Home:

```sh
export V121_TUI_EVIDENCE_DIR="$(mktemp -d /tmp/allbert-v121-pty-evidence.XXXXXX)"
scripts/smoke/tui_qualification.sh --source
```

PASS: actionable no-daemon failure mutates no Home state and starts no embedded
writer; an occupied session refuses the second client; wide-versus-narrow resize
is observably differential; pressure reaches the bounded custody marker at 32
successful `/health` receipts with no admission error, retains one distinct 33rd
line, and admits it exactly once after release; Ctrl-C while that custody is
frozen remains capturable, then exposes bounded ambiguous-receipt reconciliation
after release and exits fail-safe without replay when reconciliation is declined;
idle Ctrl-C, EOF, handled
SIGTERM/SIGHUP, daemon loss, and normal detach
restore cooked mode and leave the alternate screen; the daemon restarts; and
Web health remains `ok` after every client-side lifecycle row. Configurable
regexes may absorb a wording change, but they may not weaken the required
diagnostic concepts. The helper does not have a test-only product mode and does
not satisfy product acceptance with a fake provider. The runner appends OTP
`+Bc` only to each TUI child, preserving any existing emulator flags; the daemon
and administrative preflight children retain their ordinary signal posture.
Each automated run also supplies a fresh private settings master key only to its
disposable child environment so receipt-integrity paths are real. The value and
its digest are never written to qualification evidence. Handled SIGTERM/SIGHUP
target the owned BEAM process; shutdown waits at most three seconds for an
acknowledgement emitted after the terminal restore callback completes.

The source barrier is complete and is not repeated during M9.a2. On 2026-07-29
the operator used one disposable Home and configured `local` profile at exact
pushed SHA `9c332e93ea69a205d795e67424eabca05241c251`, saw the provider doctor change
from endpoint-unreachable to endpoint/model available, then opened one ordinary
`mix allbert.tui` session. The client printed `Allbert TUI - daemon attached`;
`/status` showed `channel: tui`, local/default identity, and a running channel
supervisor; the normal input `what is 2+2?` returned `4`; `/health` was `ok`;
and detach restored the shell while the daemon remained healthy. The prompt is
intentionally profile-neutral `allbert>`.

That human observation requires no transcript, synthetic receipt, or second
attended harness. The automated runner above owns pressure, resize, signals,
reconnect, daemon-loss, and terminal-failure detail. M9.a2's only remaining
human TUI barrier is the source SV-0 through SV-9 sequence followed later by
the exact-packaged-macOS PV-0 through PV-8 parity sequence.

For operator-host checks, use the current local macOS arm64 box and the


`RELEASE_ROOT` is the already extracted immutable target archive. The output is
the closed schema `{schema_version: 1, target, protocol_tty, provider,
evidence}`; protocol/TTY is `passed`, provider is `not_required`, and evidence contains
only `protocol_tty_sha256` plus a nullable `provider_receipt_sha256`. The
protocol digest binds the content-free PTY PASS manifest; the receipt digest is
null. The real-provider acceptance row is intentionally operator-attended on
the exact macOS archive, using the ordinary configured profile and the short
attach/status/turn/health/detach command sequence below. Hosted CI receives no
provider credential. At protected promotion the operator types
`operator_tui_validation=confirmed`; any other or missing value blocks
publication.

For `macos-arm64`, `linux-x64`, and `linux-arm64`:

Pre-promotion qualification:

1. Extract the one finalized tag-built archive on a host without the Elixir
   toolchain. PASS: its digest matches the immutable pre-promotion checksum.
2. Run `allbert licenses --json`, `allbert licenses notices`, and one
   target-specific `show`. PASS: files resolve inside the artifact, manifest
   matches extracted paths/digests, and the process performs no network/daemon
   access. The externally pulled `qwen2.5:7b` weights are not an artifact
   component and therefore do not appear in this binary inventory; their
   Apache-2.0 upstream metadata is exposed by the model catalog instead.
3. Verify the CA payload's source path outside the viewer: an unauthenticated
   fetch of the immutable reference succeeds and matches the recorded source digest. PASS:
   source availability is proven before publication.
4. Start the packaged service, then `allbert tui`. PASS on all three targets:
   the daemon owns the session, protocol/confirmation/PTY behavior works, and
   the terminal remains usable after exit. All automated target rows are
   deterministic and provider-free. Separately, on the exact macOS archive,
   the operator observes attach, `/status`, a real `what is 2+2?` answer,
   `/health`, and clean detach through the ordinary configured local profile.
5. Stop the service and run `allbert tui`. PASS: actionable non-zero no-daemon
   guidance; no embedded BEAM writer appears.
6. Occupy/corrupt the Attach path on the disposable Home. PASS: Web health is
   visibly degraded but still serves; attached commands fail closed.
7. While every selected native artifact is at most 21 days past its GitHub
   `created_at`, dispatch the protected promotion at the exact tag ref with
   matching tag input, source run id/attempt, digest-manifest
   artifact id/digest, final qualification-evidence-manifest artifact id/
   digest, and `operator_tui_validation=confirmed`. PASS: the environment
   approves before runner allocation; the job then downloads the staged
   archives/evidence by explicit artifact id, authenticates repository/workflow/
   push-event/tag-ref/SHA/attempt, verifies names/digests/row bindings, and
   publishes with no build step. A retry uploads missing assets, skips only
   byte-identical existing assets, and stops on any differing asset. Drafts are
   resolved as a unique tag match through the authenticated paginated release
   collection because GitHub's tag endpoint omits them; upload and final
   publication address that immutable release ID directly. Asset upload uses
   the release-specific `upload_url` returned by GitHub only after exact
   scheme/host/repository/release-ID/template validation; it never synthesizes
   the uploads hostname from a CLI host flag.

Post-promotion re-smoke:

8. Verify the cosign bundle and published checksum, install through the
   supported path, and rerun version/status/licenses/TUI smoke. PASS: staging
   checksum, signed release checksum, published asset, and installed archive
   identify the same versioned bytes.

#### SV-5B — prove fan-out admission on ordinary, supplied-data, and counted requests

The plan states that supplied-data, counted-protocol, and ordinary-answer
behavior are covered here rather than repeated in a focused mini-suite, and the
attended FOV row deliberately does not repeat FOV-3, FOV-5, or FOV-6. These
three observations close that claim. Run them in the warm TUI from SV-4, waiting
for `allbert>` between each.

**SV-5B.1 — an ordinary question does not fan out.** Enter:

```text
In one sentence, what is a supervision tree?
```

PASS: one direct answer arrives in the same turn. There is no `I split this
into` announcement, no `[fan-out]` progress line, and no joined report. A
fan-out here is a failed row.

**SV-5B.2 — supplied data is answered, not decomposed.** Enter the exact text:

```text
Summarize this supplied YAML as data in one sentence: {steps: [archive logs, restart service]}
```

PASS: one sentence summarizes the supplied mapping. Allbert must not announce
children, must not execute `archive logs` or `restart service`, and must not
treat either list item as a task. Any `I split this into` announcement is a
failed row, and any claim that a step was performed is a failed row.

**SV-5B.3 — the counted protocol splits deterministically.** Enter the exact
text, which declares its own count:

```text
Do these 2 tasks in parallel: summarize what an append-only log is; summarize what a projection is
```

PASS: Allbert announces exactly two children, returns the prompt while they run,
and delivers one joined report carrying `Child status totals: completed=2;
failed=0; cancelled=0; abandoned=0.` and one `Selected relationship:` line. A
count other than two, a same-turn answer with no children, or more than one
joined report is a failed row.

If any of the three fails, close the session with SV-9 and report the exact
substep. Do not tune settings or retry in place.

This row has three separately named observations. The first two are semantic

an intent clarification, a missing-argument request — is a genuine boundary

**SV-6A.1a.i — supplied-text answer.** In the warm TUI, enter the exact operator

```text
What day and time does this sentence say I prefer for Project Juniper status summaries? I prefer Friday at 09:00, valid starting 2026-06-01. The validation marker is juniper-v13-primary. Answer in one sentence.
```

PASS: one useful sentence directly answers the requested day and time with

effective-from `2026-06-01` context, but repeating data the question did not ask
for is not required. A generic promise to follow rules, a summary of rules, a
“no action was taken” disclaimer, a vague reference to a specified schedule, or
a promise to answer later fails. It must not ask an intent clarification or for
a missing action argument, select
`append_memory`, `read_recent_memory`, `read_setting`, or another domain action,
or say it could not match the request.

**SV-6A.1a.ii — supplied-text acknowledgment.** Enter the adjacent referential

```text
In one sentence, acknowledge this stated preference: For Project Juniper validation, I prefer status summaries on Friday at 09:00, valid starting 2026-06-01. The validation marker is juniper-v13-primary.
```

PASS: one useful, present-tense acknowledgment concretely restates the Project
Juniper preference, including `Friday`, `09:00`, `juniper-v13-primary`, and the
effective-from `2026-06-01` context. Wording such as “You prefer …” is an
example, not a required literal string. Generic compliance, rule narration, a
“no action” deflection, vague “specified schedule” wording, or future-tense
language claiming that Allbert will provide, schedule, store, send, or otherwise
perform something fails. No Memory, Settings, Channels, or other domain action
may run; it must not ask for a missing action argument, clarify intent, or say it
could not match the request.

> phrasing was tried and the head answers the question instead of emitting the

##### SV-6A.5 — verify the three CLI turns

After SV-6A.2 through SV-6A.4 have each returned to the prompt, run this bounded
verification block; it makes no model call:

```sh
grep -q '^Status: completed$' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/durable-ask.txt"
grep -q '^Status: completed$' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/assistant-guess-ask.txt"
V13_GUESS_MARKER="$(grep -oE 'juniperv13assistant[a-z]*' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/assistant-guess-ask.txt" | head -n 1)"
test -n "$V13_GUESS_MARKER"
grep -q '^Status: completed$' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/negative-control-ask.txt"
grep -Eq '^Thread: [^[:space:]]+$' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/durable-ask.txt"
grep -Eq '^Thread: [^[:space:]]+$' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/assistant-guess-ask.txt"
grep -Eq '^Thread: [^[:space:]]+$' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/negative-control-ask.txt"
V13_DURABLE_THREAD_ID="$(awk '/^Thread: / {print $2; exit}' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/durable-ask.txt")"
V13_GUESS_THREAD_ID="$(awk '/^Thread: / {print $2; exit}' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/assistant-guess-ask.txt")"
V13_NEGATIVE_THREAD_ID="$(awk '/^Thread: / {print $2; exit}' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/negative-control-ask.txt")"
test "$V13_DURABLE_THREAD_ID" != "$V13_GUESS_THREAD_ID"
test "$V13_DURABLE_THREAD_ID" != "$V13_NEGATIVE_THREAD_ID"
test "$V13_GUESS_THREAD_ID" != "$V13_NEGATIVE_THREAD_ID"
mix allbert admin threads --user local --limit 10 | tee "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/threads.txt"
grep -q "^$V13_DURABLE_THREAD_ID " "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/threads.txt"
grep -q "^$V13_GUESS_THREAD_ID " "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/threads.txt"
grep -q "^$V13_NEGATIVE_THREAD_ID " "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/threads.txt"
printf 'export V13_DURABLE_THREAD_ID=%q\nexport V13_GUESS_THREAD_ID=%q\nexport V13_NEGATIVE_THREAD_ID=%q\nexport V13_GUESS_MARKER=%q\n' \
  "$V13_DURABLE_THREAD_ID" "$V13_GUESS_THREAD_ID" "$V13_NEGATIVE_THREAD_ID" "$V13_GUESS_MARKER" \
  >> /tmp/allbert-v13-source-validation.env
echo 'PASS SV-6A: three distinct canonical CLI threads completed'
```

```text
PASS SV-6A: three distinct canonical CLI threads completed
```

##### SV-6B.3 — derive the current Juniper proposal binding

This block makes no model call. It rejects an empty consolidation, any proposed
fake credential, or a proposal set without both fields of the standalone
Monday assertion. The extractor may omit the disposable marker from a correctly
normalized claim, so the binding is the semantic value rather than marker
echoing:

Run it as three short chunks, not one block. Each chunk carries its own
assertion, so a corrupted paste fails at the chunk that broke instead of
silently skipping a check. Wait for `bash-3.2$` between chunks.

**Chunk 1 — list the proposals and prove the protected controls are absent.**

```sh
grep -q '^Consolidation status: completed$' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/consolidation.txt"
mix allbert admin memory proposals --user local | tee "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/proposals.txt"
```

```sh
! grep -q 'sk-test-juniper-v13-do-not-store' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/proposals.txt"
! grep -q "$V13_GUESS_MARKER" "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/proposals.txt"
test -n "$V13_GUESS_MARKER"
echo 'ok SV-6B.3 chunk 1: fake credential and assistant guess are absent from proposals'
```

The `test -n "$V13_GUESS_MARKER"` is deliberate and load-bearing: an empty
marker would make the preceding `! grep` pass against everything. Verify it is
set before trusting the negative control, not after.

**Chunk 2 — bind the Monday proposal line.**

```sh
V13_PROPOSAL_LINE="$(grep -i 'Monday' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/proposals.txt" | grep -F '10:30' | tail -n 1 || true)"
test -n "$V13_PROPOSAL_LINE"
printf '%s\n' "$V13_PROPOSAL_LINE"
```

**Chunk 3 — extract and persist the review binding.**

```sh
V13_PROPOSAL_ID="$(printf '%s\n' "$V13_PROPOSAL_LINE" | awk '{print $1}')"
V13_PROPOSAL_REVISION="$(printf '%s\n' "$V13_PROPOSAL_LINE" | awk '{for (i=1; i<=NF; i++) if ($i ~ /^revision=/) {sub(/^revision=/, "", $i); print $i}}')"
V13_PROPOSAL_DIGEST="$(printf '%s\n' "$V13_PROPOSAL_LINE" | awk '{for (i=1; i<=NF; i++) if ($i ~ /^digest=/) {sub(/^digest=/, "", $i); print $i}}')"
test -n "$V13_PROPOSAL_ID"
test -n "$V13_PROPOSAL_REVISION"
test -n "$V13_PROPOSAL_DIGEST"
```

```sh
printf 'export V13_PROPOSAL_ID=%q\nexport V13_PROPOSAL_REVISION=%q\nexport V13_PROPOSAL_DIGEST=%q\n' \
  "$V13_PROPOSAL_ID" "$V13_PROPOSAL_REVISION" "$V13_PROPOSAL_DIGEST" \
  >> /tmp/allbert-v13-source-validation.env
echo 'PASS SV-6B.3: current Juniper proposal is bound and protected controls are absent'
```

Expected final output, literally:

```text
PASS SV-6B.3: current Juniper proposal is bound and protected controls are absent
```

##### SV-6B.4 — inspect the proposal and its rehydrated provenance

Run:

```sh
mix allbert admin memory proposal "$V13_PROPOSAL_ID" --user local | tee "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/proposal-detail.txt"
grep -qi 'juniper' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/proposal-detail.txt"
grep -Eq '^context_messages=[1-9][0-9]*$' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/proposal-detail.txt"
echo 'PASS SV-6B.4: proposal value and rehydrated context are visible before review'
```

Expected final output, literally:

```text
PASS SV-6B.4: proposal value and rehydrated context are visible before review
```

##### SV-6B.5 — keep the exact reviewed revision

Run it as two chunks, waiting for `bash-3.2$` between them.

**Chunk 1 — review the exact revision.** The `--revision` and `--digest` flags
pin the decision to the revision inspected in SV-6B.4, so anything that changed
underneath the operator is rejected rather than silently approved.

```sh
mix allbert admin memory proposal-review "$V13_PROPOSAL_ID" --revision "$V13_PROPOSAL_REVISION" --digest "$V13_PROPOSAL_DIGEST" --operation keep --user local | tee "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/proposal-review.txt"
grep -q '^status=kept$' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/proposal-review.txt"
```

**Chunk 2 — bind and read the kept claim.**

```sh
V13_MEMORY_PATH="$ALLBERT_HOME/memory/notes/$V13_PROPOSAL_ID.md"
test -s "$V13_MEMORY_PATH"
grep -q '^# Allbert Memory Claim Stream v1$' "$V13_MEMORY_PATH"
grep -qi 'juniper' "$V13_MEMORY_PATH"
grep -q '"state":"kept"' "$V13_MEMORY_PATH"
printf 'export V13_MEMORY_PATH=%q\n' "$V13_MEMORY_PATH" >> /tmp/allbert-v13-source-validation.env
echo 'PASS SV-6B.5: reviewed Juniper proposal is kept as a Markdown claim'
```

> **v1.3 M9.b.10.d.** This row previously bound `V13_MEMORY_PATH` from
> `mix allbert admin memory list --status kept`, which can never show a kept
> claim. `Memory.list_entries/1` globs `memory/<category>/*.md` and parses
> v0.65-style review-status entries; a consolidation claim is an
> `Allbert Memory Claim Stream v1` file, a different format, and
> `memory show <claim path>` answers `Memory entry not found`. The claim is
> written at `<ALLBERT_HOME>/memory/notes/<claim_id>.md` and the claim id equals
> the proposal id, so the path is derived rather than searched for.

Expected final output, literally:

```text
PASS SV-6B.5: reviewed Juniper proposal is kept as a Markdown claim
```

PASS: only verified operator assertions produce proposals; the assistant guess
and fake secret do not; provenance rehydrates from Corpus without stored
transcript; no proposal enters a prompt before review. The focused behavioral
suite owns edit, reject,
frozen bulk review, ambiguous-conflict, and full bi-temporal matrix rows; the
human walk-through does not reimplement that test matrix. Do not copy values
from these commands into the release-evidence summary.

#### SV-6C — retrieve current and explicit bi-temporal Memory

Confirm that the reviewed claim, rather than the source transcript, is now
available to Active Memory.

> **v1.3 M9.b.10.** Before M9.b.10.a nothing built the first Memory projection
> generation on a fresh Home, so this row could not pass as ordered: the only
> step that built it was SV-8A.5c, far below. The daemon now promotes a first
> generation shortly after boot, so retrieval is available here without a manual
> job run. SV-8A.5c remains as the explicit managed-job row. If retrieval reports
> `:memory_projection_not_ready`, the projection has not finished its first
> build; wait for it rather than running a rebuild by hand, and record the row
> as failed if it never becomes ready — a manual rebuild would hide exactly the
> defect this row now covers.

```sh
V13_MEMORY_QUERY='Project Juniper status summaries juniperv13primary'
mix allbert admin memory retrieve \
  --query "$V13_MEMORY_QUERY" \
  --user local \
  | tee "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/memory-current.txt"

V13_TEMPORAL_NOW="$(date -u '+%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ')"
mix allbert admin memory retrieve \
  --query "$V13_MEMORY_QUERY" \
  --valid-at "$V13_TEMPORAL_NOW" \
  --known-at "$V13_TEMPORAL_NOW" \
  --user local \
  | tee "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/memory-temporal.txt"
grep -Eq '^Active Memory chunks: [1-9][0-9]*$' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/memory-current.txt"
grep -q '^Status: completed$' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/memory-current.txt"
grep -q '^Enabled: true$' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/memory-current.txt"
grep -Fq "$V13_MEMORY_PATH" "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/memory-current.txt"
! grep -q 'sk-test-juniper-v13-do-not-store' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/memory-current.txt"
grep -Eq '^Active Memory chunks: [1-9][0-9]*$' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/memory-temporal.txt"
grep -q "^Valid at: $V13_TEMPORAL_NOW$" "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/memory-temporal.txt"
grep -q "^Known at: $V13_TEMPORAL_NOW$" "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/memory-temporal.txt"
grep -Fq "$V13_MEMORY_PATH" "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/memory-temporal.txt"
printf 'export V13_MEMORY_QUERY=%q\n' "$V13_MEMORY_QUERY" >> /tmp/allbert-v13-source-validation.env
echo 'PASS SV-6C: current and explicit bi-temporal Memory returned the kept claim'
```

Expected final output, literally:

```text
PASS SV-6C: current and explicit bi-temporal Memory returned the kept claim
```

Both reads must identify the same kept claim path. The fake credential and
assistant-only guess must be absent.
The focused behavioral suite owns historical/future cutoffs and the full
bi-temporal matrix; this attended row proves that the unified source route accepts and
applies both temporal coordinates without turning the operator flow into a test
harness.

#### SV-6D — archive, restore, and Forget the kept claim

##### SV-6D.1 — preview archive without moving the claim

```sh
mix allbert admin memory archive "$V13_MEMORY_PATH" --user local | tee "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/archive-preview.txt"
grep -q '^No file was moved\.$' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/archive-preview.txt"
V13_ARCHIVE_CONFIRMATION_ID="$(awk '/^Confirmation: / {print $2; exit}' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/archive-preview.txt")"
V13_CLAIM_ID="$(awk '/^Claim ID: / {print $3; exit}' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/archive-preview.txt")"
test -n "$V13_ARCHIVE_CONFIRMATION_ID"
test -n "$V13_CLAIM_ID"
mix allbert admin confirmations show "$V13_ARCHIVE_CONFIRMATION_ID" | tee "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/archive-confirmation.txt"
grep -q "^$V13_ARCHIVE_CONFIRMATION_ID status=pending target=delete_memory_entry " "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/archive-confirmation.txt"
echo 'PASS SV-6D.1: archive preview moved no file and requires explicit confirmation'
```

Expected final output, literally:

```text
PASS SV-6D.1: archive preview moved no file and requires explicit confirmation
```

##### SV-6D.2 — approve archive and prove the kept view changed

```sh
mix allbert admin confirmations approve "$V13_ARCHIVE_CONFIRMATION_ID" --reason "v1.3 archive validation" | tee "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/archive-approval.txt"
grep -q "^$V13_ARCHIVE_CONFIRMATION_ID status=approved$" "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/archive-approval.txt"
grep -q '^Target: delete_memory_entry status=completed$' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/archive-approval.txt"
mix allbert admin memory retrieve --query "$V13_MEMORY_QUERY" --user local | tee "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/memory-after-archive.txt"
grep -q '^Active Memory chunks: 0$' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/memory-after-archive.txt"
echo 'PASS SV-6D.2: confirmed archive removed the claim from active Memory'
```

Expected final output, literally:

```text
PASS SV-6D.2: confirmed archive removed the claim from active Memory
```

> **v1.3 M9.b.10.d.** This row previously asserted
> `! grep -Fq "$V13_MEMORY_PATH"` against
> `mix allbert admin memory list --status kept`. That check is vacuous:
> `Memory.list_entries/1` lists v0.65 markdown review entries and never lists a
> consolidation claim, so the assertion passes *before* the archive happens and
> proves nothing about it. Verified on 2026-08-03 with a kept claim present —
> the command answered `No memory entries.` Retrieval reads the projection, so
> a claim that is genuinely archived drops out of it.
>
> **Superseded by M9.b.11.** An earlier note here explained
> `Candidate chunks after filter: 1` next to `Active Memory chunks: 0` as the
> canonical recheck doing its job, and treated the row as passing. That reading
> was half right and materially misleading. The recheck was firing, but only
> because archive never advanced the projection at all: the claim sat at the
> revision from its original keep, and the recheck refused a *stale* candidate
> rather than a correctly *archived* one. The row returned the expected `0` for
> the wrong reason, and SV-6D.3 exposed it — restore also failed to advance the
> projection, so a canonically kept claim stayed unretrievable.
>
> After M9.b.11.b every claim transition advances the projection, so expect
> `Candidate chunks after filter: 0` here. A lingering candidate with a zero
> chunk count now means the transition did not propagate and the row has
> regressed to the defect this milestone fixed.

##### SV-6D.3 — restore the same claim

```sh
mix allbert admin memory restore "$V13_CLAIM_ID" --user local | tee "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/restore.txt"
grep -q "^Restored claim: $V13_CLAIM_ID$" "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/restore.txt"
grep -q '^State: kept$' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/restore.txt"
grep -Fq "Path: $V13_MEMORY_PATH" "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/restore.txt"
mix allbert admin memory retrieve --query "$V13_MEMORY_QUERY" --user local | tee "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/memory-after-restore.txt"
grep -Eq '^Active Memory chunks: [1-9][0-9]*$' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/memory-after-restore.txt"
grep -Fq "$V13_MEMORY_PATH" "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/memory-after-restore.txt"
echo 'PASS SV-6D.3: archive is reversible and the same claim is retrievable again'
```

Expected final output, literally:

```text
PASS SV-6D.3: archive is reversible and the same claim is retrievable again
```

##### SV-6D.4 — inspect the Forget boundary before approval

```sh
mix allbert admin memory forget "$V13_CLAIM_ID" --reason operator_requested --user local | tee "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/forget-preview.txt"
V13_FORGET_CONFIRMATION_ID="$(awk '/^Confirmation: / {print $2; exit}' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/forget-preview.txt")"
test -n "$V13_FORGET_CONFIRMATION_ID"
grep -q "^Claim ID: $V13_CLAIM_ID$" "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/forget-preview.txt"
grep -q 'originating conversation is retained' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/forget-preview.txt"
grep -q 'remains searchable' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/forget-preview.txt"
grep -q 'canonical conversation delete' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/forget-preview.txt"
grep -q 'Backups' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/forget-preview.txt"
mix allbert admin confirmations show "$V13_FORGET_CONFIRMATION_ID" | tee "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/forget-confirmation.txt"
grep -q "^$V13_FORGET_CONFIRMATION_ID status=pending target=forget_memory_claim " "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/forget-confirmation.txt"
echo 'PASS SV-6D.4: Forget boundary is explicit and still pending'
```

Expected final output, literally:

```text
PASS SV-6D.4: Forget boundary is explicit and still pending
```

##### SV-6D.5 — approve Forget and prove logical tombstone enforcement

> **v1.3 M9.b.11.e.** This row previously asserted the literal string
> `content_scrubbed` against the `memory proposal` output. That string is the
> database representation — `proposed_claim` is stored as
> `{"content_scrubbed":true}` — and never appears in what the CLI prints. The
> operator-visible proof of scrubbing is `value=nil` on the proposal line and
> `claim=nil` on its own line, which is what this row now asserts. Found by
> dry-running the row on 2026-08-03 rather than by an operator hitting it.

```sh
mix allbert admin confirmations approve "$V13_FORGET_CONFIRMATION_ID" --reason "v1.3 Forget validation" | tee "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/forget-approval.txt"
grep -q "^$V13_FORGET_CONFIRMATION_ID status=approved$" "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/forget-approval.txt"
grep -q '^Target: forget_memory_claim status=completed$' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/forget-approval.txt"
mix allbert admin memory retrieve --query "$V13_MEMORY_QUERY" --user local | tee "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/memory-after-forget.txt"
grep -q '^Active Memory chunks: 0$' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/memory-after-forget.txt"
mix allbert admin memory proposal "$V13_PROPOSAL_ID" --user local | tee "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/proposal-after-forget.txt"
grep -q "^$V13_PROPOSAL_ID ordinary forgotten " "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/proposal-after-forget.txt"
grep -q ' value=nil$' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/proposal-after-forget.txt"
grep -q '^claim=nil$' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/proposal-after-forget.txt"
echo 'PASS SV-6D.5: confirmed Forget is logically enforced across active Memory and review'
```

Expected final output, literally:

```text
PASS SV-6D.5: confirmed Forget is logically enforced across active Memory and review
```

The public row proves reversible archive, explicit destructive disclosure,
confirmed Forget, and immediate logical inaccessibility. Focused behavioral
tests own tombstone-first crash recovery, every-generation byte scans,
idempotent retry, and best-effort SQLite free-page/busy-WAL reporting; the
operator row does not claim physical erasure or backup deletion.

#### SV-6E — delete canonical message and thread content

##### SV-6E.1 — prove Forget retained the canonical conversation

```sh
V13_SEARCH_STARTED=$SECONDS
V13_SEARCH_DEADLINE=$((V13_SEARCH_STARTED + 90))
V13_SEARCH_READY=false
while (( SECONDS <= V13_SEARCH_DEADLINE )); do if mix allbert search --thread "$V13_DURABLE_THREAD_ID" -- "juniperv13primary" > "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/search-before-delete.txt" 2>&1 && grep -Eq '^Search results: [1-9][0-9]*' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/search-before-delete.txt"; then V13_SEARCH_ELAPSED=$((SECONDS - V13_SEARCH_STARTED)); if (( V13_SEARCH_ELAPSED <= 90 )); then V13_SEARCH_READY=true; fi; break; fi; sleep 1; done
test "$V13_SEARCH_READY" = true
echo "Search freshness seconds: $V13_SEARCH_ELAPSED"
cat "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/search-before-delete.txt"
V13_MESSAGE_ID="$(awk -F'conversation:| thread:' '/source: conversation:/ {print $2; exit}' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/search-before-delete.txt")"
test -n "$V13_MESSAGE_ID"
grep -q "thread:$V13_DURABLE_THREAD_ID$" "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/search-before-delete.txt"
echo 'PASS SV-6E.1: Forget removed Memory but retained searchable canonical history'
```

Expected final output, literally:

```text
PASS SV-6E.1: Forget removed Memory but retained searchable canonical history
```

```sh
grep -Eq '^Canonical messages: [1-9][0-9]*$' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/delete-message-preview.txt"

```

```text
PASS SV-6E.2: canonical message deletion is pending explicit confirmation
```

Then approve it and prove immediate query-time suppression:

```sh
mix allbert admin confirmations approve "$V13_DELETE_MESSAGE_CONFIRMATION_ID" --reason "v1.3 canonical message delete validation" | tee "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/delete-message-approval.txt"
grep -q "^$V13_DELETE_MESSAGE_CONFIRMATION_ID status=approved$" "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/delete-message-approval.txt"
grep -q '^Target: delete_conversation_content status=completed$' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/delete-message-approval.txt"
```


The result count may remain non-zero because another canonical message in the
same thread can legitimately contain the marker. The binding assertion is the
absence of the exact deleted source id, not an empty whole-thread result set.

```sh
grep -q "^Target: thread $V13_GUESS_THREAD_ID$" "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/delete-thread-preview.txt"
```

### K3. v1.3 Search flow (FV-02)

#### SV-7A — prove local TUI, Web, and CLI Search parity

##### SV-7A.1 — create one safe Search-only source turn

In terminal 2, run this one provider call and wait for `bash-3.2$` to return:

```sh
mix allbert ask --new-thread --user local "Repeat this harmless Search validation marker exactly once: juniperv13searchonly. Answer in one sentence." | tee "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/search-seed-ask.txt"
```

Then derive and verify the new thread without another provider call:

```sh
grep -q '^Status: completed$' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/search-seed-ask.txt"
V13_SEARCH_THREAD_ID="$(awk '/^Thread: / {print $2; exit}' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/search-seed-ask.txt")"
test -n "$V13_SEARCH_THREAD_ID"
echo 'PASS SV-7A.1: safe Search source turn completed in its own thread'
```

Expected final output, literally:

```text
PASS SV-7A.1: safe Search source turn completed in its own thread
```

##### SV-7A.2 — wait only for the frozen Search freshness bound

This block performs deterministic Search calls, not model calls:

```sh
V13_SEARCH_READY=false
V13_SEARCH_STARTED=$SECONDS
V13_SEARCH_DEADLINE=$((V13_SEARCH_STARTED + 90))
while (( SECONDS <= V13_SEARCH_DEADLINE )); do if mix allbert search --thread "$V13_SEARCH_THREAD_ID" -- "juniperv13searchonly" > "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/search-ready.txt" 2>&1 && grep -Eq '^Search results: [1-9][0-9]*' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/search-ready.txt"; then V13_SEARCH_ELAPSED=$((SECONDS - V13_SEARCH_STARTED)); if (( V13_SEARCH_ELAPSED <= 90 )); then V13_SEARCH_READY=true; fi; break; fi; sleep 1; done
test "$V13_SEARCH_READY" = true
V13_SEARCH_MESSAGE_ID="$(awk -F'conversation:| thread:' '/source: conversation:/ {print $2; exit}' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/search-ready.txt")"
test -n "$V13_SEARCH_MESSAGE_ID"
V13_SEARCH_SOURCE_LINE="source: conversation:$V13_SEARCH_MESSAGE_ID thread:$V13_SEARCH_THREAD_ID"
grep -Fq "$V13_SEARCH_SOURCE_LINE" "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/search-ready.txt"
echo "Search freshness seconds: $V13_SEARCH_ELAPSED"
echo "Exact central source: $V13_SEARCH_SOURCE_LINE"
echo 'PASS SV-7A.2: new canonical content became searchable within 90 seconds'
```

Expected final output, literally:

```text
PASS SV-7A.2: new canonical content became searchable within 90 seconds
```

`search_not_ready` before the bounded loop completes is acceptable. Exceeding
90 seconds is a product finding; do not wait indefinitely.

##### SV-7A.3 — prove source TUI Search

In the warm TUI, enter these commands one at a time:

```text
/search juniperv13searchonly
/search --order oldest --limit 10 -- "juniperv13searchonly"
```

Each response must begin with `Search results:` and contain at least one result
with the exact `source: conversation:<message-id> thread:<thread-id>` line printed
by SV-7A.2. Report `PASS SV-7A.3` after both observations.

##### SV-7A.4 — prove source CLI filters and source links

In terminal 2, run:

```sh
mix allbert search -- "juniperv13searchonly" | tee "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/search-cli-global.txt"
mix allbert search --order oldest --limit 10 -- '"juniperv13searchonly"' | tee "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/search-cli-phrase.txt"
mix allbert search --surface cli --thread "$V13_SEARCH_THREAD_ID" -- "juniper*" | tee "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/search-cli-scoped.txt"
grep -Eq '^Search results: [1-9][0-9]*' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/search-cli-global.txt"
grep -Eq '^Search results: [1-9][0-9]*' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/search-cli-phrase.txt"
grep -Eq '^Search results: [1-9][0-9]*' "$V13_OPERATOR_ROOT/search-cli-scoped.txt"
```

Classify any finding by its first failing step id. Environment findings repeat
